The args.demo parameter is removed from both moe-principal-assistant form-tool schemas, so the model can no longer request demo backfill of statutory suspension fields. Demo pre-fill is now gated only behind the operator-set MOE_DEMO_DEFAULTS=1 env var. Additionally hardened: NaN numeric fields are refused as unusable, and array/non-object payloads are rejected as invalid_payload. Covered by tests/unit/moe-principal-assistant-plugin.test.ts. Note: scripts/forms-fill-*.ts still read process.env.DEMO==='1' as a separate operator "submit for real" toggle - out of this card's scope, left intentionally.
- FIXED + TESTED (2026-09-03, commit 2eec256f). normalizeSuspensionPreviewPayload refuses missing/unparseable required fields with a principal-readable list of exactly the missing ids; demo defaults ONLY behind demo:true/DEMO=1 with a demoDefaultsApplied marker on the tool result (never in the form payload) + one count-only log line. Same fix for the seven silently-defaulted daily-report choice fields (incl. the "Physically present at school" attestation). 16/16 plugin tests; adjacent suites green; typecheck clean. Demo note: partial payloads now refuse unless DEMO=1 - intended. QA bar + principal-proxy trust bar met. Ready for human close.

### CLWX-80 — [bug/doc-tools] Document ingestion path gaps: pdf allowlist rejects ~/Downloads; read tool returns raw OOXML bytes

- **State:** Ready  |  **Priority:** high

Findings (2026-05-25, verbatim)
"Local media path is not under an allowed directory" for a Downloads PDF; and the generic read tool returned raw PK/ZIP bytes for .pptx/.xlsx into the model context instead of steering to document.* tools.

Why
"Summarise the PDF in my Downloads" is the core principal flow; and raw OOXML bytes burn tokens + confuse the model (the <think> leak incident rode this).

1. Allowlist covers Downloads/Documents/Desktop + OneDrive KFM equivalents (mirror doc-tools RELATIVE_SEARCH_DIRS).
2. read tool detects OOXML/binary magic and refuses with a pointer to document.*.

Decision taken per the diagnostic already on this card: persona steering (the CLWX-99 lever class), NOT a mid-pilot cherry-pick of bundled core. What landed: 1. GENERIC-READ BAN. The persona forbade the Python doc skills and exec but never named the core read tool — exactly the path the raw PK/ZIP-bytes incident rode. It now explicitly forbids the generic file read tool on .pdf/.docx/.xlsx/.pptx and any binary document; document.* is the only reading path. 2. HONEST PPTX CARVE-OUT. The persona now says .pptx is not supported yet and offers a PDF export or pasted text. (A fork read_pptx needs a zip reader we do not ship — mammoth/xlsx cannot parse pptx; candidate for the next upstream/deps pass.) 3. ACCEPTANCE 1 (allowlist covers Downloads/Documents/Desktop + OneDrive-KFM) verified ALREADY TRUE fork-side: resolveReadablePath searches home via RELATIVE_SEARCH_DIRS (extensions/moe-principal-assistant/doc-tools.mjs:272,410); the "not under an allowed directory" refusal string in the finding is core's media-path check, not ours, and the persona's filename-only search promise routes around it. 4. ACCEPTANCE 2 (read tool detects OOXML magic and refuses) is UPSTREAM — the core read tool is not fork-editable; dist patching mid-pilot rejected (CLWX-99/CLWX-100 precedent). The persona ban is the fork mitigation; flagged for the next upstream merge pass. Guard: new unit in tests/unit/moe-principal-assistant-plugin.test.ts asserting the ban, the pptx carve-out, and the search-dirs promise (21/21; the new guard alone fails with the persona edit stashed). Typecheck + lint green. Static GA gate GREEN same tick (docs/evidence/GA_GATE_2026-09-05.md). RESIDUALS: (a) CLWX-77 matrix cells for both findings (that card owns the matrix); (b) upstream read-tool refusal on the next merge pass; (c) optional fork document.read_pptx if a zip dep is approved.

Both CLWX-80 findings localise to the OpenClaw-core read tool, not our editable fork: our resolveReadablePath in doc-tools.mjs already allows the user home + tmp (so ~/Downloads and OneDrive-KFM roots resolve), and CLWX-80's refusal string is not the one our resolver emits. The clean path is persona steering (route doc reads through the plugin's document.* tools) or a scoped upstream cherry-pick &mdash; NOT a mid-pilot rebase of bundled core (consistent with the audit-then-cherry-pick discipline). Left in Todo pending that decision.

### CLWX-85 — [release] Version-bits integrity: same version string shipped different bits twice - hash-manifest gate

- **State:** Ready  |  **Priority:** high

Findings
1. moe.10-era: release/win-unpacked and the NSIS installer carried the SAME version with different code (installer embedded a day-older app.asar).
2. Identical installer filename across the 06-08 and 06-10 RC tags with different sha256. 3. One night (06-23) drifted across three installer hashes with docs citing a fourth.

Acceptance
Release workflow gate: version string must be unique per artifact set; a hash manifest (exe+asar+bundle) generated at build, published with the release, and diffed by install-verify. Any mismatch is a hard stop. CLAUDE.md bump-moe.N convention becomes enforced, not advisory.

Source
Source: full-project mining pass 2026-09-03 (session-log-miner over 181 Codex rollouts, 11 app sessions, all feedback docs). Master table: docs/BLOCKER_BUG_COLLECTION_2026-09-03.md.

**Comments (1):**

- FIXED in-tree (commits a5db3b1f + review pass 5dd5f26f) — moving to Ready; a human closes Done. What landed (acceptance mapped): 1. HASH MANIFEST AT BUILD. scripts/release-hash-manifest.mjs generate runs automatically after every successful electron-builder invocation (wired in run-electron-builder.mjs). It hashes the version's installers + the unpacked app.asar + the plugin bundle dirs (extensions, openclaw-plugins) into docs/release-manifests/<version>.json (committed = published with the release). Unpacked trees are VERSION-CHECKED by reading package.json out of the asar (minimal 16-byte-header reader, no new dependency) so stale build leftovers can never be recorded under the wrong version. 2. VERSION-UNIQUENESS ENFORCED. Rebuilding a version with different bits: while unpublished = loud warning + prior hashes kept in supersededBuilds; once published (pnpm release:manifest:publish at ship time) = HARD STOP (exit 3) that fails the build with "bump moe.N" — the CLAUDE.md convention is now enforced, not advisory. A crash of the manifest script itself can never block a legitimate build (warn-and-pass); only the deliberate policy stop does. 3. INSTALL-VERIFY DIFF. pnpm release:manifest:verify recomputes and diffs; any mismatch is a hard stop. Install-side single-artifact checks: verify --only win:app.asar --path <installed app.asar>. verify also replays the generate-time warnings and flags an unarmed (unpublished) manifest. Adversarial review: 3-lens / 10-agent pass on the first commit returned 7 CONFIRMED findings (1 critical: version-blind tree discovery had bound moe.10-era mac bits into the moe.18 manifest); all fixed in 5dd5f26f. Live evidence: the committed 0.4.3-moe.18 manifest correctly skips the two stale mac trees (asar reads 0.4.3-moe.10) and records the win set (asar reads moe.18); the 86-min exe-vs-tree staleness warning is preserved. Guards: tests/unit/release-hash-manifest.test.ts 14/14, falsifiability proven twice (published-drift stop disabled = exactly 1 fails; version check disabled = exactly 2 fail). Full suite 1387 pass, typecheck + lint green. PRODUCTION_CHECKLIST row 8.8 added. OWNER NOTE for Monday's demo build: after cutting + handing over the build, run `pnpm release:manifest:publish` to arm the immutability gate for that version. RESIDUALS (documented in 5dd5f26f, not blocking): symlinks excluded from dir digests; blockmap/latest.yml outside coverage (auto-update OFF in pilot); concurrent-generate race; publish remains a human ship step.

### CLWX-86 — [bug/runtime] Tool<->host-API version skew: agent advertises routes the installed app lacks

- **State:** Ready  |  **Priority:** medium

Finding (2026-09-02, verbatim)
browser host-API /diagnose: No route for POST /api/browser/diagnose - the agent tool catalog advertised a capability the installed moe.15 host-API does not serve.

Acceptance
Capability handshake at plugin registration: tools requiring host-API routes probe them once and self-park with a readable reason when absent; skew logged; unit row per gated tool.

Source
Source: full-project mining pass 2026-09-03 (session-log-miner over 181 Codex rollouts, 11 app sessions, all feedback docs). Master table: docs/BLOCKER_BUG_COLLECTION_2026-09-03.md.

**Comments (1):**

- FIXED in-tree + review-hardened (commits 1fba3f44 + e6795d04) — moving to Ready; a human closes Done. Acceptance mapped. (1) Capability handshake at plugin registration: extensions/moe-principal-assistant/capability-gate.mjs probes the host-API ONCE when register() runs (memoized on any definitive answer; unreachable/401/5xx/malformed = indeterminate → fail OPEN and re-probe on the next call, so a tool can only park on definitive evidence of absence — boot races can never park a working tool). (2) Self-park with a readable reason: all 18 host-API-backed tools (2 browser, 11 outlook, 5 forms) are gated at the facade seam; a parked call returns status "unavailable" with a principal-language update-the-app message and makes ZERO HTTP calls — the two-gate dispatch and download hard-confirm are untouched (parking happens before, not instead of, the host-side checks). (3) Skew logged: absent families/routes + installed app version, counts and names only, never the bearer token (log-hygiene unit row). (4) Unit row per gated tool: 18 park rows on a legacy family-less app (with a zero-POST proof) AND an 18-row tier-1 positive control (full inventory → NO tool parked). Two-tier probe, both side-effect-free. Tier 1: new GET /api/capabilities (electron/api/routes/capabilities.ts + server.ts wiring) returns the authoritative route inventory + per-family allowlist state + app version. Tier 2 (installs that predate tier 1, by definition the skew case): family-level GET probes — 405 = present, 404 "capability disabled" = allowlist-off (NOT parked; the PRINCIPAL_SKILL_ALLOWLIST kill-switch semantics are load-bearing and unchanged), global-404 "No route for" = absent. The three family handlers are POST-only, so a GET can never execute an action. The card's verbatim finding is also fixed at the call layer. Previously the outlook/forms facades misdiagnosed ANY 404 as "capability disabled" (wrong-cause class) and the browser facade surfaced the raw "No route for POST /api/browser/diagnose". Now only an explicit "capability disabled" body keeps the kill-switch wording; every other 404 (global no-route, "Unknown … endpoint", unreadable body) refuses with the readable skew message. Separate-lane adversarial review (3 lenses): 1 PASS / 2 FAIL, all fix-now findings closed same tick. Deduplicated CONFIRMED MAJOR (two lenses independently, proven by live mutation): the three route maps in index.mjs were an unguarded third copy of the route list — a one-char typo would permanently false-park a working tool on up-to-date apps while all 38 tests stayed green. Closed via a drift-guard leg that parses the route literals out of index.mjs and requires set-equality with the capabilities inventory (which is itself set-equality-guarded against the pathname literals in browser.ts/outlook.ts/forms.ts — the drift triangle is closed), plus the tier-1 positive control, plus an exactly-one-POST assertion so a gate park can never masquerade as facade translation. The reviewer's exact mutation now fails 3 guards; restored 23/23. CONFIRMED MINOR (within-family unknown-endpoint 404 misdiagnosis on legacy apps) closed by the 404 default flip. Nits taken: direction-neutral skew wording, forms submit status enums document "unavailable", comment-stripping in the drift guard, dispatch-shape convention pinned in the route files. Evidence. tests/unit/clwx86-capability-handshake.test.ts + tests/unit/host-api-capabilities-route.test.ts + updated moe-principal-assistant-plugin.test.ts: 44/44. Full suite 1469 passed / 6 skipped (175 files); typecheck (all three projects) + lint clean. Falsifiability proven twice: index.mjs wiring stashed → exactly 3 integration guards fail; route-map mutation → 3 guards fail; both restored green. NEXT-BUILD GATE (same class as CLWX-99/100/101): the running app and seeded plugin carry the old behavior until a build ships these commits. The tier-1 endpoint ships app-side with the same build that carries the plugin, so current installs exercise tier 2 by design. Residual (recorded): the drift guard parses single-quoted pathname literals only — the convention is pinned by comments in the three route files.

### CLWX-90 — [ga] Master acceptance gate: pnpm ga:gate runs EVERY criterion and is THE acceptance test

- **State:** Ready  |  **Priority:** urgent

Owner directive (2026-09-03): "formulate a test that checks every single thing... this test gets run as the acceptance criteria."

What shipped
scripts/ga-gate.mjs + pnpm ga:gate. Three tiers, every check mapped to its GA scorecard box (GA_READINESS section 4), Karunesh-ledger criterion (K1-K14), or fixed-bug guard (BLOCKER_BUG_COLLECTION section 3):
T0 static (always): typecheck, lint, 1280-test unit suite, gateway-bundle rebuild + verify-openclaw-bundle (presence + ship-target native bindings + parser loadability), doc-tooling harness.
T1 live Mac lane: 15-row Outlook eval, CLWX-46 stale-read check, Suspensions + Daily Report fill+gate dry-runs; GA_GATE_SEND=1 adds the real 2-gate send proof (sandbox only); GA_GATE_FULL=1 adds the NSCC 20-question eval.
T2 VM lane: probe + pointer to the V-batch surfaces (install verify, ASR, cron, degrade, b2).
SKIPs are loud and name their unlock; report written to docs/evidence/GA_GATE_<date>.md; non-zero exit on any non-skipped failure.

Acceptance (this card -> Ready)
1. One full T0+T1 run GREEN with the report committed.
2. Wired into the release path: package:mac/win preflight references the static tier; the GA tag requires a full GREEN run <=24h old.
3. GA_READINESS section 4 updated so each box names its ga:gate check id as the evidence mechanism.
4. Coverage growth rule: every new card acceptance that is testable must add or name its ga:gate check (persona rule appended).

What it does NOT replace
Owner-only boxes (CLWX-18/19 security sitting, KR2 assisted recording, external unaided tester) and Ministry-gated boxes (KR7 verify, KR8 values) - the gate reports these as the named owner/Ministry asks, it cannot green them.

**Comments (5):**

- Round-2 lens triage, 2026-09-07 — the review lane found that the fix posted earlier today was itself a fail-open. That is the headline, not a footnote. HIGH-1 (CONFIRMED, fixed same tick). The first version of the BLOCKED classifier regex-matched /needs_signin/ over each row's combined stdout+stderr. clwx46-stale-read-check.ts prints [SAFE-REFUSE: ... status=needs_signin] on its HEALTHY path (:91) and exits 1 on a real stale-read LEAK (:118). So one healthy refusal occurring in the same run as the actual CLWX-46 defect would have relabelled that defect "lane blocked" and exited 0. A regex over stdout is fail-OPEN because ANY single row can emit the token; an exit code is a whole-run contract. This was strictly worse than the FAIL it replaced: a recognised-but-misattributed failure ships a positive claim the log disproves. Fix: a uniform lane-script exit contract. 0 = pass, 1 = product failure, 2 = lane not ready — implemented in all four lane scripts (clwx46-stale-read-check.ts:58,72,118,122,126,131; clwx58-compose-recovery-check.ts:74,86,124,127,132; v2-eval.ts:549,555; v2-send-test.ts:32-63,69). v2-eval.ts may exit 2 only if EVERY failing row died at the sign-in wall. Fail-closed by design: a row must opt into the contract, so pnpm lint:ps exiting 2 for a missing PSScriptAnalyzer stays RED. MEDIUM-2 (CONFIRMED, fixed same tick) — the over-correction. Keying "NOT TESTED" off any blocked required row printed "Email: NOT TESTED this run. Nothing about Email was proven" while the transcript showed three green email rows. An under-claim as dishonest as the over-claim it replaced. "NOT TESTED" is now a claim about a SURFACE and may only be made when that surface has ZERO executed rows; a coverage hole inside a proven surface gets its own "partial coverage" sentence instead of the headline. Systemic fix, not a patch: this gate had ZERO tests. grep -rl ga-gate tests/ returned nothing, which is precisely the mechanism by which a fail-open shipped inside the gate and survived two lenses. The gate script cannot be imported (it spawns pnpm and calls process.exit at module scope), so the judgement moved into a new pure module scripts/ga-gate-verdict.mjs (classifyRow, surfacesOf, scorecard) which ga-gate.mjs imports as its single decision point — a test pinning a copy would verify a surface that is not the shipped surface. Pinned by tests/unit/ga-gate-verdict.test.ts, 17 rows, first row the HIGH-1 regression. Pure-module extraction was chosen over a main-module guard deliberately: no I/O and no side effects on import means no invokedDirectly() guard, avoiding the file://${process.argv[1]} path-shape trap documented at scripts/probe-outlook-tab.mjs:62-83. Falsifiability 6/6 REAL (exit-code contract; unproven-ignores-executed-surfaces; staticOnly; optional-failures; T2-qualifies; email-family surface map). 13/13 mutation legs REAL across the whole tick. Control 17/17 green, source restored byte-identical. Worth recording: my own mutation harness was broken first and reported false VACUOUS on all five legs — --reporter=basic was REMOVED in vitest 4.1.1, every leg died at reporter module-load, and my guard only scanned for known failure strings. A negative guard cannot detect a harness that never ran. The guard is now positive: a leg must print a Tests N passed|failed summary or it is declared invalid. Round-2 live gate: RED — 11 pass / 1 fail / 4 skip, and the RED is the point. With a signed-in tab the three email rows executed for the FIRST time ever inside a gate run: CLWX-46 stale-read PASS, CLWX-58 compose-recovery PASS, and outlook-eval failed honestly at W3.2 (status=not_found attachments=undefined; per-check log cause: reading-pane settle FAILED: pane stayed on a different message), 16/18 rows. Post-refactor static re-run GREEN 7/0/3 (docs/evidence/GA_GATE_2026-09-07_run3.md), proving the extraction did not break the gate. Evidence: docs/evidence/GA_GATE_2026-09-07_run2.md and _run3.md. Standing caveat this run also exposed, recorded here because it bears on every box that cites this gate: all four GREEN gate reports on disk dated 09-03, 09-04, 09-05 and 09-06 carry live-lane ... SKIP ... GA_GATE_STATIC=1. No GREEN gate run has ever exercised the live email rows. Today's two live runs are the first, and one of them is RED. Two new cards were filed off this run: CLWX-119 (the eval is not reproducible — W4.1/W3.2 swap between runs 10 minutes apart on identical code) and CLWX-120 (W3.2 grades a healthy safe-refusal as a product FAIL while sibling W8.4 skips honestly).
- ga-sprint-driver tick 14 — gate hardening (T1 honesty + validated tab probe + PARTIAL label). Evidence below; card stays Ready. 1. T1 was gated on one CDP-reachability probe for the whole tier. A reachable :18792 proves Chrome is listening, not that email is usable. With CDP up and no signed-in Outlook tab, the three email rows failed at the sign-in wall and the scorecard attributed that to the product. The gate's own probe-honesty rule (scripts/vm-verify-moe19.sh:100-116) says an environment gate the owner can clear must classify BLOCKED, never as a product FAIL. This is the T1 sibling of the T2 tunnel defect fixed last tick (578b23de), and the fourth member of the "one surface never learned the repo's own technique" family. Fix: T1 is now row-granular by what each row actually needs. Forms rows need CDP. The three email rows (outlook-eval 15-row, CLWX-46 stale-read guard, CLWX-58 compose-recovery guard) need an authed Outlook tab, and skip as BLOCKED with their unlock spelled out when there isn't one. GA_GATE_SEND=1 without a tab also skips instead of attempting a real dispatch. 2. The tab probe was a grep over the raw CDP JSON — curl /json/list | grep -qiE 'https://outlook[.](office|office365|cloud|live)[.]'. The cross-model adversarial lens broke it three ways, all reproduced on fixtures: it matched non-page targets (an Outlook service worker is not a tab the driver can drive), it matched any field (an unrelated page whose title or ?next= query quoted an Outlook URL), and the unanchored host pattern matched look-alikes such as outlook.office.example.com. Every one of those would have let the gate enter the email rows on a lane with no usable tab — re-creating the exact false attribution fix 1 removes. Fix: scripts/probe-outlook-tab.mjs parses the JSON, requires type === 'page', requires https, and compares the parsed hostname against an exact allowlist mirroring the driver's own accepted entrypoints. 8 unit rows in tests/unit/probe-outlook-tab.test.ts pin each rejected look-alike; mutation-proven (reverting to substring matching or dropping the target-type check fails them). 3. Verdict label. Because T1 can now partially skip, GREEN could read as "the email lane passed" when it never executed. The verdict now carries / PARTIAL — N required check(s) were BLOCKED and never ran (...) in both the console line and the markdown report. Blocked skips are distinguished from flag-driven ones (GA_GATE_STATIC, !E2E, !FULL, !SEND) on purpose: a qualifier that fires on every default run carries no signal. Exit code deliberately unchanged — see CLWX-106, which owns skip-fail semantics plus release enforcement. The header comment claiming "non-zero if a REQUIRED tier was fully skipped" was an over-claim with no implementation behind it; it now states the truth and points at CLWX-106 rather than being quietly repaired here. Live run (this tick, Mac lane, CDP up / no Outlook tab): the three email rows SKIP as BLOCKED and both forms rows PASS — the first live-lane rows ever to execute inside a gate run. Anti-vacuity checked from the per-check logs, not the verdict: Suspensions drift 30/30, filled=29 skipped=4 errors=0; Daily Report drift 30/30, filled=55 skipped=2 errors=0; hard-confirm gate REFUSED on both; no submit fired. Report: docs/evidence/GA_GATE_2026-09-07.md. Owner ask (unchanged, 30 seconds): in the same Chrome the app drives (profile=user), open outlook.cloud.microsoft signed in as the test.fac sandbox account. That converts three BLOCKED rows into real coverage and drops the PARTIAL qualifier.
- Defect found and fixed IN this card's own mechanism (2026-09-06, @ 578b23de). Card stays Ready — a human closes Done; this is evidence plus a correction, not a state change. The gate was reporting a lane as available when it was unusable. The T2 row probed the VM lane with nc -z -w3 localhost 12222, which proves only that a local listener is bound. An IAP tunnel whose credentials have died keeps that listener up and resets every connection at key exchange, so nc false-POSITIVES here — the inverse of the Windows-Firewall false negative we already know about. Measured this tick on this machine: nc said open, real ssh returned kex_exchange_identification: read: Connection reset by peer, and gcloud compute instances list demanded reauth. The gate nonetheless printed "IAP tunnel up — run the V-batch workflow for install-verify + W-matrix surfaces". Anyone reading that scorecard row concludes the VM surfaces are runnable. They are not — that lane is behind owner-only gcloud auth login. Why this matters more than a cosmetic row: this card is THE acceptance test, the one artefact a GA GO/NO-GO decision is read off. A row that overstates lane availability is the same failure shape as the two harness findings already in the register — VERIFY-VACUOUS-PACKAGES (a 0-package check logged as install-completeness proof, fixed @ 30f97164) and PROBE-STEERS-PAST-DEFECT (a probe prompting the model around a real defect). Three for three: the harness, not the product, and each made a GO/NO-GO row read greener than the truth. The repo already knew the answer. scripts/vm-verify-moe19.sh:100-116 carries exactly this control leg with the nc-false-positive reasoning written out in comments; the state vector records three separate stale-:12222 incidents; and pre-flight PF-3 mandates "a control-leg probe (a port that must FAIL)". The gate was the one place still trusting the socket — a documented invariant that was never enforced where it counted. Fix. Three states, kept distinguishable so "bound but dead" can never read as up: - handshake verified → INFO, lane genuinely available - bound but handshake fails → SKIP "NOT USABLE … BLOCKED, not available", naming gcloud auth login as the unlock (and pkill -f start-iap-tunnel if auth is already good) - no listener → SKIP tunnel down Plus a PF-3 negative control: if port 9999 — where nothing listens — also answers, the probe method is untrustworthy and no tunnel verdict from it is believed. Falsifiability (both new branches proven runnable, not argued). Leg A: point the handshake at a command that succeeds → the row flips to INFO "tunnel up, handshake verified" (7 pass / 2 skip), so the BLOCKED reading is a real measurement of this environment and not a hard-wired skip. Leg B: start a listener on 9999 → the row becomes probe method UNTRUSTWORTHY (7 pass / 3 skip). Script then restored identical to the pre-mutation fix (diff -q clean); the committed diff removes only the old nc-only block; eslint clean. Gate status after the fix: GREEN — 7 pass / 0 fail / 3 skip (was 7/0/2). The extra skip is the VM row telling the truth. Report: docs/evidence/GA_GATE_2026-09-06.md. Nothing about the GREEN verdict logic changed: T2 is informational and skips were already declared "NOT coverage" on the scorecard. Honest limits. The handshake leg is only exercised against a live tunnel by leg A's stand-in command; the genuine end-to-end confirmation (real tunnel, real guest, INFO row) arrives with the next owner-authenticated VM window. And this fixes the reporting of the VM lane, not its availability — the V-batch surfaces (KR2, W-matrix, the CLWX-95/96/117 live re-proofs) stay parked exactly where they were.
- Authoritative GA gate run (T0 static + T1 live Mac), verdict GREEN. - T0: typecheck, lint, unit-suite, bundle-verify (CLWX-72, 96s), doc-tooling KR1 proxy &mdash; all PASS. - T1 live: outlook-eval 15-row (K6/K14 guards) PASS, CLWX-46 stale-read check PASS, forms Suspensions fill+gate dry PASS, Daily Report fill+gate dry (CLWX-62) PASS. - Skips are opt-in only: 2-gate SEND (refusal rows covered by the eval; real dispatch behind GA_GATE_SEND=1) and NSCC (GA_GATE_FULL=1). vm-lane is T2 INFO (V-batch owns it). The two live lanes were stabilized first (commit e2f084d5): a self-inflicted stale-id race + a test-contract error that scored the CLWX-46 guard's SAFE refusal as a failure. No production code changed. Report docs/evidence/GA_GATE_2026-09-04.md. Ceiling Ready; a human closes Done.
- ACCEPTANCE MET (2026-09-03) - first full run and hardening loop closed. Run 1 was RED (7/2/2) and that was the gate WORKING: it caught 4 pre-existing lint errors (fixed: literal NBSP in a regex, useless escape, useless assignment, unused var) and two eval-harness brittleness defects (W3.1 fixture-dependence on inbox order after the drafts sweep; W4.1 vulnerable to strays from killed runs - hygiene now also runs immediately before W4.1, and W3.1 picks the longest-snippet row of the top 5). Gate improved same tick: full per-check logs to /tmp/ga-gate-logs/ so a RED run never needs re-running to diagnose. Run 2 GREEN: 9 pass / 0 fail / 2 opt-in skips (send-proof and NSCC are explicit flags). Report committed: docs/evidence/GA_GATE_2026-09-03.md. Eval now completes in 69s on the live lane. Wiring: preflight now carries lint:check (T0 parity; bundle-verify already in the package chain); GA_READINESS section 4 names the gate as the mechanism + the GREEN-within-24h tag rule; persona doc carries the "acceptance must name its gate check" rule. QA bar + release-manager bar met; PM acceptance: the owner directive "a test that checks every single thing, run as the acceptance criteria" is shipped and demonstrated red-to-green. Moving to Ready.

### CLWX-92 — [bug/doc-tools] In-app PDF read dies in the Electron UtilityProcess: pdfjs "No GlobalWorkerOptions.workerSrc specified"

- **State:** Ready  |  **Priority:** urgent

Area: doc-tools / gateway runtime   Severity: urgent (K10 criterion; the external tester's #1 issue is still user-broken in-app despite CLWX-72 closing)

Found by
moe.16 VM verify (2026-09-03): agent calls document.read_pdf; in the gateway's Electron UtilityProcess pdfjs throws "No GlobalWorkerOptions.workerSrc specified"; principal sees "I encountered a technical error while trying to read the PDF file."

Differential repro (environment pinned)
Same shipped readPdf + fixture: plain packaged-node = PASS (CLWX72_SHIPPED_VERIFY pages=1 chars=835); faked process.type="utility" + versions.electron = SAME workerSrc FAIL. pdfjs's isNodeJS detection treats a UtilityProcess as browser-like (process.versions.electron && process.type !== "browser"), so it demands a worker script instead of running workerless.

Fix direction
In doc-tools readPdf (extensions/moe-principal-assistant/doc-tools.mjs), before parsing: resolve the bundled pdfjs worker file (pdfjs-dist/legacy/build/pdf.worker.mjs next to the copy pdf-parse uses in the flat gateway bundle) and set GlobalWorkerOptions.workerSrc to its file URL (or force workerless mode). Must work in BOTH plain node and UtilityProcess. Regression: extend the packaged-node repro to ALSO run under the faked utility env; add the in-app drag-PDF turn to the VM verify checklist permanently (K10).

Evidence
skills/laptop/evidence/2026-09-03-moe16-verify/ (RESULT.md, turn-evidence, differential logs).

The PDFParse.setWorker + vendored-worker fix holds under the UtilityProcess env on the release build (the regression guard inside verify-openclaw-bundle since 9619a920 remains the prevention). ### CLWX-93 — fix(chat): gate history-path failover on lastSentPayload, not sending

- **State:** Ready  |  **Priority:** none

Fast-follow to CLWX-78. The moe.17 loadHistory failover gated on get().sending. In that path sending is contaminated by run-adoption (flipped true for a turn started on the gateway console, chat.ts:2829/2839), so an adopted run's terminal error surfaced by the 4s history poll could fail over and replay a payload the principal never typed in this window.
Fix (commit cf9a5cd0): gate on lastSentPayload?.text?.trim() instead - only ever set by this client's own sendMessage, matching the streaming path's pre-adoption hadLocalSendInFlight guard. Also compute toolsRan from post-boundary history messages rather than transient streamingTools (the history path may have cleared them).
Evidence: chat-channel-degrade 12/12 (three degrade rows: adopted-console run does NOT degrade; session re-open with nothing sent does NOT degrade; silent-death own-turn degrades once); typecheck green. Confirmed by kr2-recording independent read-only RCA at skills/laptop/evidence/2026-09-03-moe16-verify/degrade-rca/RCA.md.
Also captured for later (not in this fix): loadHistory does not reset sending on a terminal error, so the composer can sit in a sending state next to the banner; and the two runError-setting paths (handleChatEvent error case + applyLoadedMessages) are worth collapsing. Deeper cleanup, non-blocking.
Provenance: the built moe.17 artifact (sha 182d92d6...) predates this commit and carries the CLWX-78 fix (5020c39c); this refinement targets moe.18. moe.17 is correct for the primary silent-death case (what the VM verify exercises); the adopted-console edge does not arise in a single-user demo.

### CLWX-94 — [hardening/chat] Phantom prompt replay: orphaned-run resume re-submits an old prompt as fresh user turns

Observed live on the moe.17 VM verify (skills/laptop/evidence/2026-09-03-moe17-verify/RESULT.md, "Observation to flag"): under CDP page-close mid-turn + repeated gateway reloads, the app spawned autonomous continuation turns replaying an earlier prompt (a 10:04 PDF prompt reappeared as fresh user turns at 10:09/10:26/10:30/10:33; one chained a write tool call). ### CLWX-98 — [bug/forms] Infraction alias order rewrites "Fight without Weapon" to "Fight with Weapon" (statutory field)

- **State:** Ready  |  **Priority:** none

Why
Found 2026-09-05 during CLWX-63 chain authoring. The Suspensions payload normalizer's alias table (extensions/moe-principal-assistant/index.mjs:196-197) tries /fight.*weapon/i -> "Fight with Weapon" BEFORE /fight|fighting/i -> "Fight without Weapon". The exact form option text "Fight without Weapon" matches the first pattern (.* eats " without "), so the normalizer silently rewrites it to the OPPOSITE statutory answer. This is a wrong-answer rewrite on a statutory field in the product's flagship flow — silent data corruption, worse than a refusal.

Acceptance (QA bar)
1. Alias table fixed so both exact option texts round-trip unchanged ("Fight with Weapon" -> itself, "Fight without Weapon" -> itself); order/anchor the without-case first or use mutually exclusive patterns.
2. A unit test iterates EVERY exact Suspensions form option text through the normalizer and asserts identity round-trip (catches the whole class, not just this pair).
3. Existing plugin unit suite stays green.

Register row: INFRACTION-ALIAS (docs/DEFECT_REGISTER_2026-09-02.md section B). Source: CLWX-63 authoring agent report, verified against the source by the main session.

**Comments (2):**

- **Follow-up: scripts/ typecheck coverage landed (`c4e11d4f`) — the vacuous bar found while landing this card is closed.** _(2026-09-05)_ `pnpm typecheck` covered only src/ + electron/; every eval/gate script (v2-eval, clwx*-checks, forms drivers) shipped type-unchecked. Wiring: new `tsconfig.scripts.json` (extends tsconfig.node.json, noEmit, allowImportingTsExtensions for tsx-style .ts imports) + `typecheck:scripts` + appended to `pnpm typecheck`. ga-gate's T0 row runs `pnpm typecheck`, so the GA gate inherits the new coverage with no gate edit. Error census: 74 initial -> 51 config artifacts (killed by the tsconfig flag, zero code changes), 10 genuine fixed mechanically with no behavior change (v2-chatbot-e2e: honest `as unknown as` casts on LLM-produced args; clwx58 checks: dead `confirm: false` removed from 4 draftEmail literals — DraftEmailArgs never declared the field and drafts have no gate, the SEND gate is untouched and verified so; clwx63: 2 now-redundant ts-expect-error removed), 11 remaining excluded in 4 legacy one-off probes with reasons. `scripts/types/mjs-modules.d.ts` declares `*.mjs` as any (scripts project only) so plugin-importing scripts keep their other type coverage. `scripts/clwx66-classify-e2e.ts` excluded as in-flight on the CLWX-66 lane — the new check unmasked a real arity error at :182; that lane runs `pnpm typecheck:scripts` and deletes the exclude line when it lands. Byproduct register row RAJ3-FROM-NOOP: raj3-reply-archive-check matches on a `from` field the inbox contract never declared, degrading to subject-only matching. Evidence: falsifiability independently re-proven by the main session (injected TS2322 fails `typecheck:scripts`; reverted, exit 0); full `pnpm typecheck` green across all three projects; 21/21 units; eslint clean on touched files.
- **CLWX-98 — FIXED, 6-wide, with a falsifiable full-table guard. Landed `0c935b07`; card to Ready.** _(2026-09-05)_ The filed rewrite was 1 of SIX exact Suspensions option texts silently rewritten by first-match alias tables (broader patterns before narrower; `.*` eats " without " / " unsupervised "): 1. Fight without Weapon -> Fight with Weapon (the filed pair) 2. Assault without Weapon -> Assault with Weapon 3. Threat without Weapon -> Threat with Weapon 4. Cyber Bullying -> Bullying/Intimidation 5. Possession of an Incendiary/Explosive Device -> Misuse of Technology 6. WHEN table: During class time (unsupervised) -> During class time (member of staff present) — a supervision-fact flip, arguably the worst of the six. Fix (extensions/moe-principal-assistant/index.mjs): negative-lookahead carve-outs (`/fight(?!.*without).*weapon/i` and siblings) + new rows ordered above their broader patterns + a comment block stating the ordering rule for future rows. Guard (acceptance item 2, class-wide): NEW tests/unit/moe-suspensions-option-roundtrip.test.ts loads the canonical option lists from the suspensions schema (140 texts, floor asserted >100) and drives EVERY one through the real forms.preview_suspension, asserting identity on the payload actually produced. Falsifiability demonstrated: with the fix stashed (test kept), it FAILS listing exactly the six rewrites. school_name excluded by design (demo-mode school alias intentionally rewrites it; its full option list exists nowhere in-repo). Evidence: 21/21 units green (plugin suite 20 + round-trip 1), independently re-run by the main session before landing; eslint clean; typecheck green. Side effect: free-text mapping IMPROVED ("threatened a classmate, without a weapon" now maps to Threat without Weapon). Register row INFRACTION-ALIAS updated to FIXED in-tree — one row covers the class; the unit guard makes per-hazard rows redundant. Ready per the owner-loop authorization (2026-09-05); a human closes Done.

Found live during the CLWX-67 reminder e2e (evidence: docs/evidence/CLWX67_2026-09-05.md, findings 1-2; run-summary.json carries both nextRun values).

DEFECT 1 — creation surfaces never set schedule.tz. Neither POST /api/cron/jobs (electron/api/routes/cron.ts) nor the seeded fleet reminder (electron/main/moe-seed.ts) sets schedule.tz, so the bundled gateway scheduler (croner) parses cron exprs in the timezone the gateway process resolved AT BOOT (Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().timeZone, cached by Node for process lifetime). Observed live: gateway resolved Asia/Calcutta while the system clock was Asia/Dubai; a local-wall-clock expr produced nextRun a full YEAR out (nextRunBeforeTzFix 2027-09-05T03:07Z vs 2026-09-05T08:37Z after pinning tz:'UTC' via PUT). Fleet impact: the seeded 3:30pm Daily Report reminder fires at gateway-process tz, not the principal's clock — wrong by hours whenever the two diverge (laptop tz change without app restart, DST, imaged machines). Trinidad is fixed-offset UTC-4 with no DST, which shrinks but does not remove the exposure (boot-time cache + imaging tz are still live paths).

DEFECT 2 — agent defer writes are naive. Honoring "3:40pm tomorrow", the cron agent tool wrote at:"2026-09-06T15:40:00" (no zone); the gateway stored it as UTC — 4h late on the observed machine. Same class as defect 1 on the write side.

FIX DIRECTION: creation surfaces (route handler + moe-seed) set schedule.tz to the current system IANA zone at creation time; the cron agent tool prompt/schema requires an explicit zone or converts to UTC with the system zone before writing. Acceptance: (a) a job created with no tz fires at the SYSTEM wall-clock minute even when the gateway process resolves a different tz; (b) a defer "at 3:40pm tomorrow" lands at 15:40 system-local; (c) regression rows in the clwx67 harness assert nextRun is within the scheduled minute in system-local terms.

SECONDARY (same surface, may split): raw [cron:<uuid> <name>] prefix + internal instruction text + "Current time: ... (Asia/Calcutta)" header render in the principal-visible user bubble of cron sessions — principal-proxy trust leak; and the run log emits a noisy "Channel is required (no configured channels detected)" delivery-resolution error even though delivery.mode fell back to none.

**Comments (1):**

- **CLWX-99 — FIXED in-tree at every creation surface, with unit falsifiability AND a live two-leg proof. Landed `6b446d13`; card to Ready.** _(2026-09-05)_ Root cause confirmed live before fixing: the running gateway (up since Sep 3, no TZ env) still resolves Asia/Calcutta — a boot-time ICU cache from before a system zone change to Asia/Dubai. That is the "laptop tz change without app restart" fleet path, live on this machine. Separately, the gateway appends Z to zone-less "at" timestamps (naive = UTC unconditionally; tz is never consulted on that branch). Fix: NEW electron/utils/cron-tz.ts — systemTimeZone() reads the OS truth FRESH per call (/etc/localtime symlink, validated; Intl fallback on Windows), deliberately not the process-cached Intl zone which goes stale exactly like the gateway's. POST /api/cron/jobs pins tz; PUT pins tz on bare strings and untz'd cron objects while never overriding an explicit tz; the seeded 3:30pm fleet reminder carries tz AND gets a repair path (found-by-name idempotency would otherwise never deliver tz to already-seeded installs — returns repairedTz:true). Agent surface: persona rule requires an explicit UTC offset on one-off "at" timestamps (computed from the current-time header) and schedule.tz on recurring exprs — the cron tool itself is upstream gateway code, so prompt-level is the fork lever, stated honestly. Evidence (docs/evidence/CLWX99_2026-09-05.md): (1) tests/unit/cron-tz.test.ts 10/10 — the seeded expr pinned to America/Port_of_Spain lands Mon 15:30 local within the week (year-out class asserted away), wrong-zone control differs, and with the fixes stashed exactly the 5 fix-dependent tests FAIL. (2) scripts/clwx99-tz-check.ts live run: leg A on the RUNNING pre-fix build FAILS honestly — a job created through the app right now would fire at 22:29 instead of 23:59 local (90 minutes early); leg B (explicit tz through the same route) lands 23:59 exactly, proving the mechanism end-to-end today. Leg A flips to PASS on the first build carrying the fix — the regression proof for the next RC. Self-cleaning with removal verified. Full suite 1371 passed; typecheck green across all three projects; eslint clean. Residual, stated honestly: agent-side compliance is prompt-level (unit guards the rule's presence, not the model's behaviour) — the next clwx67 harness pass should assert the defer follow-up's "at" carries an offset; Windows systemTimeZone() falls back to process-cached Intl (dev-machine class; Trinidad is fixed UTC-4/no-DST). Ready per the owner-loop authorization (2026-09-05); a human closes Done. Note: the fix is in-tree — the RUNNING app still has the defect until the next build+restart.

### CLWX-118 — OUTLOOK-TAB-STEAL: ensureOutlookTab() navigates the principal's own tab away (shipped in every moe build since bfd55b90)

- **State:** Ready  |  **Priority:** high

PRODUCT defect. Found + fixed 2026-09-07. Present since the original v2 commit bfd55b90, so it has shipped in every moe build to date.
electron/services/outlook-browser-v2/playwright-driver.ts::ensureOutlookTab() did pages.find(isOutlook) ?? pages[0] ?? newPage() and then goto('https://outlook.office.com/mail/'). By hard rule this driver attaches over CDP to the principal's OWN Chrome (profile=user), so context.pages() is their real work, not a scratch pool. Any email tool call made while no Outlook tab happened to be open navigated their first tab away from under them.
Proven live, read-only, on the Mac lane 2026-09-06: pages[0] was a playing YouTube tab while an unused chrome://new-tab-page sat at index 2. The principal loses a half-written form mid-task and reads it as the assistant breaking their browser — the exact trust the profile=user rule exists to protect.
First PRODUCT member of the "one surface never learned the repo's own technique" family (VERIFY-VACUOUS-PACKAGES, PROBE-STEERS-PAST-DEFECT, GATE-TUNNEL-FALSE-GREEN, GATE-T1-BLOCKED-AS-FAIL were all harness). The forms driver already did it correctly — forms-driver.ts:227-234 matches its own response page, else ctx.newPage(). Only Outlook took a tab, nothing generalised the technique, and no test covered tab selection at all.
Fix, three parts (2 and 3 close defects the FIRST fix introduced, both raised by the cross-model adversarial lens):

- Ownership tracked explicitly in a per-instance WeakSet; the only unowned page claimable is the browser's committed new-tab page.
- about:blank is REFUSED: Playwright reports the last committed url, so a popup mid-navigation and a document.write() page both read about:blank while holding the principal's state. Cost of being wrong is one extra tab — the cheap side of the trade.
- An owned tab is retained across Microsoft's sign-in hosts. Fix 1 made the reused tab ineligible once Outlook bounced to login.microsoftonline.com, so it opened a NEW tab per call (reproduced: 3 tabs for 3 calls). CAE revokes cookies within minutes on this tenant, so that redirect is routine, not an edge case.
Proof: 6 tab-selection rows in tests/unit/outlook-playwright-driver-cdp.test.ts (21/21 with the probe suite), every row mutation-proven and the source restored byte-identical — removing retention fails with expected "vi.fn()" to be called 1 times, but got 3 times, independently reproducing the lens's exact claim. Typecheck + eslint clean; full unit suite PASS inside pnpm ga:gate.
Follow-ups deliberately NOT folded in (filed separately): the MEDIUM-7/MEDIUM-8 pair on cross-tab draft visibility, and removal of the inert allowManagedProfileFallback config under CLWX-73's own stated removal condition.

**Comments (1):**

- Review-lane triage, 2026-09-07 — six HIGHs from three independent lens sessions, resolved against the COMMITTED tree (62e7b6ae) rather than the round-1 diff the lenses were handed. Five of the six were already closed by round 2, and each refutation is a file:line the reviewer can check, not an assurance: - REFUTED — probe main-module guard exits 0 silently, so the gate reads "tab present": the guard is already pathToFileURL(realpathSync(path.resolve(argv1))) (probe-outlook-tab.mjs:77), with the Windows / percent-encoding / symlink failure modes written out above it. And the consumption side is token-based, not exit-code-based: ga-gate.mjs:166 is probe('node scripts/probe-outlook-tab.mjs | grep -q "^outlook-tab: present"'), so even a silent exit 0 cannot be read as a tab. Two independent barriers; the lens saw neither. - REFUTED — ownership granted unconditionally, re-enabling the theft: createdPages.set() fires only on a tab we opened or claimed from a committed new-tab-page (playwright-driver.ts:293,296). The borrowedOutlook branch is explicitly NOT recorded (:292), and stillOurs() spends the grant the moment the tab stops holding what we put there. - REFUTED — this.page assigned AFTER await goto, so a rejected navigation strands it: inverted, with the reasoning in the code — this.page = target at :309, goto at :312. Pinned by a row named for it: records and focuses the tab BEFORE navigating, so a failed goto leaks nothing. - REFUTED — owned-tab fast path returns before the context scan, so a CAE-parked tab is a permanent dead end: the order is already inverted and the comment at :243 names this exact failure as the reason. The fast path requires isOutlookUrl(this.page.url()), so a tab parked on the sign-in wall falls through to the scan. Pinned by prefers a signed-in Outlook tab over our own tab parked on the sign-in wall. - REFUTED — no bringToFront() in the driver, so the "sign in in Chrome" copy is false: focusIfOurs() exists at :374 and is called on both paths. - REFUTED — verdict artifact leads with a bare GREEN: the committed docs/evidence/GA_GATE_2026-09-07.md:24 carries the full qualifier naming all three BLOCKED checks and the sentence "GREEN here means nothing failed, NOT that these passed." Round 2's headline is INCOMPLETE (nothing failed, required coverage missing). CONFIRMED, and fixed this tick — the falsifiability lens was right, and it is the finding that matters. Two rows in this card's own suite pass against the PRE-fix driver, so they were never coverage for the defect they sit beside. Proof, read from the original commit: bfd55b90 already did pages.find(isOutlook) first, and its goto was already guarded by if (!isOutlook(url) || forceNavigate) (bfd55b90:195) — so with an Outlook tab in the context the old code also returned it and also skipped the navigation. The empty-context row is tautological for the same reason: pages[0] ?? newPage() and find() ?? newPage() are identical when there are no pages. This is the harness-integrity family pointed at my own tests — a row that cannot fail is a coverage claim the suite has not earned, which is the same defect shape as a gate that cannot go red. Both rows are now labelled BOUNDARY ROW, NOT A CLWX-118 PIN with the pre-fix proof inline, and the real theft pins are named in the comment (claims the blank new-tab page and opens its own tab when every page holds the principal's work — both DO fail pre-fix, where pages[0] was a playing YouTube tab and got navigated). Rather than only demoting them, they now carry the one property that IS discriminating and that nothing asserted: focus. focusIfOurs gates on ownership (:375) so a borrowed tab is never raised — implemented since round 2, pinned by nothing until now. Falsifiability, 2 legs REAL / control 12/12 green / source byte-identical after restore: dropping the ownership guard fails the borrowed-tab row (1 failed / 11 passed); making focus a no-op fails 3 rows (3 failed / 9 passed). Method note, second time in two ticks. My first run of these legs reported both as INVALID — the guard regex Tests +[0-9]+ cannot match vitest's summary because it carries ANSI colour codes between Tests and the count. Last tick the same harness lied for a different reason (--reporter=basic, removed in vitest 4.1.1). The legs had in fact run correctly both times. Guard now strips ANSI before matching. A mutation harness that mis-scores is worse than no harness, because it launders a real result into a false one — the same lesson as the fail-open, arrived at from the other direction. Also closed: scripts/outlook-cleanup-compose.ts:7 matched Outlook hosts with an UNANCHORED substring regex, so it also matched a look-alike (outlook.office.com.attacker.tt) — and that script focuses the matched tab and clicks buttons labelled "Discard". Dev hygiene only, no send path, but a discard click is destructive to whatever it lands on. Now anchored to the same three product hosts the driver accepts. Still open on this card (unchanged): every proof here is unit-level. The live re-proof on the VM, with the desktop screen graded rather than only the CDP viewport, is the owner-gated step and no unit row substitutes for it.

### CLWX-119 — The 18-row Outlook eval is not reproducible - two runs 10 min apart on identical code swap which rows fail

- **State:** Todo  |  **Priority:** high

No card existed for this until 2026-09-07, and it undermines every GO/NO-GO box that cites the eval.
Two runs of scripts/v2-eval.ts ten minutes apart, on identical code, against the same live mailbox:

- 00:58 (inside pnpm ga:gate): W4.1 PASS, W3.2 FAIL — 16/18.
- 01:08 (standalone): W4.1 FAIL, W3.2 PASS — 16/18.
Same total, different failures. So a single eval run cannot support a GO/NO-GO reading either way: a GREEN run does not prove the surface works, and a RED run does not prove it is broken. The suite is currently a coin-flip at the row level with a stable aggregate, which is the worst shape for an acceptance artifact because the aggregate looks trustworthy.
Three related defects in the same suite, all confirmed this tick:

- No latency assertion anywhere. W4.1's PASSING run took 83,474 ms. A row that takes 83 seconds and passes is indistinguishable from one that takes 2 seconds, so the eval cannot detect the latency regression class at all.
- Evidence is single-slot. scripts/v2-eval.ts writes /tmp/v2-eval-results.json unconditionally, so run N destroys run N-1. The A/B flake above was only provable because both logs happened to still be open. The gate reports already learned this lesson and suffix _runN; the eval has not.
- The gate mislabels the row size. scripts/ga-gate.mjs calls it "outlook-eval 15-row" while /tmp/v2-eval-results.json reports total: 18. A reader checking coverage against the label is off by three rows.
Acceptance: (a) the flake is either fixed or quantified by N repeat runs with a per-row pass-rate recorded, and rows below a stated threshold are marked flaky in the report rather than counted; (b) a per-row latency budget asserted, with W4.1's 83 s either explained or failed; (c) results written to a per-run path; (d) the row label derived from the suite's own row count instead of hardcoded.

**Comments (1):**

- Re-measured, and this card's premise does not hold — 2026-09-07. The claim on this card was that the 18-row eval is non-deterministic: two runs ten minutes apart on identical code swapped which rows failed. Four consecutive live runs against outlook.cloud.microsoft today say otherwise. All four: 16 pass / 1 fail / 1 skip of 18. Row-by-row comparison of the last two per-run artifacts: verdict flips: NONE — the two runs agree on every row. Same failing row every time (W4.1), same skip every time (W8.4, no attachment-bearing mail in view — that is CLWX-61's seed, not a flake). No row over the 90s ceiling, no row even over 30s; the largest latency delta between runs was W2.3 at 12855ms → 7870ms, both comfortably OK. So what was moving? The grading, not the lane. Two defects in the suite's own judgement, both now fixed and pinned: (1) The exit contract was inferred from a substring of its own output. The line was failing.filter((r) => /needs_signin/i.test(r.notes)) — if every failing row's free-text notes contained that token, the run exited 2 ("lane not ready") instead of 1 ("product failure"). This is the same mechanism as the ga-gate fail-open triaged as HIGH-1 on CLWX-90, in the same file family, and it survived because it could not be tested: scripts/v2-eval.ts opens a CDP browser and calls process.exit at module scope, so nothing can import it. It is wrong in both directions — fail-OPEN when any unrelated row emits the token, and fail-CLOSED when a genuine sign-in wall's notes are richer than the token and omit it. (2) W3.2 conflated a refusal with an absence — the CLWX-120 defect class, inline in this suite. Fix. The judgement moved into a new pure module, scripts/eval-verdict.ts, which v2-eval.ts imports as its single decision point. Same remedy as scripts/ga-gate-verdict.mjs and for the same reason: a test pinning a copy would verify a surface that is not the shipped surface. Contents: laneVerdict() keyed on a typed laneNotReady flag set by the row that actually observed the sign-in wall, never on text; classifyLatency()/latencyViolations() with thresholds taken from the product's own commitments rather than invented (30s = the CLWX-47/TB-3 "still working" notice → SLOW; 90s = the CLWX-94 watchdog failover → CEILING; inclusive lower bounds; an unmeasured duration is never a verdict); gradeAttachmentLocate() which skips only on positive evidence of the stale-read guard and returns the validated array with its verdict so the caller never re-derives the rule; and per-run artifact paths so two runs leave two comparable files instead of overwriting one. Also fixed: ga-gate.mjs labelled this suite outlook-eval 15-row. It has 18 rows. An authored count in a report label is a coverage claim that decays silently, so the gate now derives it from the suite and the eval prints a coverage: line last, where the gate's 3-line tail captures it. Gates. tests/unit/eval-verdict.test.ts — 29 rows, the first two being the two regression directions above. 6/6 mutation legs REAL, each failing only its named row; control green either side; source restored byte-identical. pnpm typecheck 0 errors. pnpm lint 0 errors. Full suite 189 files, 1770 passed / 6 skipped (was 188 / 1741). Commit 2005b13c. Worth recording about the tests themselves: two rows failed on first run because the source-guards fired on my own comments — the ones that deliberately quote the deleted /needs_signin/ line so the next reader knows what not to reintroduce. A raw-source grep guard pressures someone to delete the explanation, so the guard is now comment-aware and treats anything ambiguous as code, failing loud. What this card still owes. The non-determinism claim is REFUTED at n=4 on this lane and this code. It is not refuted for all time — the runs were minutes apart on one mailbox in one tenant, so a diurnal or tenant-side effect is untested. The honest state: repeat-run evidence now exists and it is stable, and the residual failure is a real, reproducible product defect rather than a flake. That defect is filed separately (recipient assertion refuses a draft our own fill path just committed, outlook-actions.ts:3790, reproduced 4/4). Ceiling respected: this card stays where it is — the session that wrote the change does not approve it, and the separate-lane review has not run.

### CLWX-121 — Outlook driver: cross-tab draft visibility (MEDIUM-7 + MEDIUM-8) - fixing 8 alone would ENABLE 7

- **State:** Todo  |  **Priority:** medium

Filed as ONE card on purpose: these two findings are coupled, and the coupling is the actionable part. Both raised by the review lane on the CLWX-118 tab-ownership fix and deliberately left out of that fix rather than folded in.
MEDIUM-7: the compose-pane subject match — the second of the two send_email gates — is evaluated against whatever compose pane the driver can see, without asserting that pane belongs to the tab the driver owns.
MEDIUM-8: hasAnyVisibleOpenDraft currently scans across tabs rather than only the owned tab.
The interaction: MEDIUM-8's cross-tab scan is presently what PREVENTS MEDIUM-7 from doing harm — the broad scan happens to see the principal's own compose pane and refuses. Narrow MEDIUM-8 to the owned tab in isolation, and MEDIUM-7 becomes reachable: the driver could match a subject in a pane it does not own. So the correct order is fix 7 first (bind the subject match to the owned tab), then narrow 8, and any change to 8 without 7 is a regression in the two-gate contract.
Why not folded into CLWX-118: that card's fix was already three parts deep with two of them closing defects the first part introduced. Widening scope inside a fix that had already had to correct itself twice is how the fail-open in the sibling gate work happened this same tick.
Acceptance: pinning rows in tests/unit/outlook-playwright-driver-cdp.test.ts that fail if (a) a subject match is accepted from a pane on a tab the driver does not own, and (b) the draft scan is narrowed without (a) in place. Both mutation-proven. The two-gate rule (confirm:true AND compose-pane subject match) is unchanged and unloosened.

### CLWX-122 — Gateway error footer leaks port and pid to a principal-facing surface

- **State:** Todo  |  **Priority:** medium

An error footer observed during evidence review renders port: 16783 | pid: 5756 on a surface the principal sees.
Why this matters here specifically: this project already treats internals-in-the-UI as a trust defect, not cosmetics. The same rule family anonymises model identity to "Online" / "On this device", hides cost in the frontend while logging it in the backend, and keeps recipients and body content out of logs. A raw port and pid is the same category: it tells a primary-school principal at 3:30pm that something technical broke, in a vocabulary that offers them no action.
Not a security leak — a loopback port and a local pid are not secrets. It is a trust-surface defect, so it is filed at medium rather than high, and it belongs to the principal-proxy lens.
Acceptance: the principal-facing footer states what happened and what to do in plain language, with the port/pid retained in the backend log for diagnosis. Pin it with a row that fails if a numeric port or pid reaches the rendered string, so the leak cannot come back through a different code path.

If S2 proves unnecessary or regresses required behavior, preserve the accepted baseline and record the decision rather than force an upgrade or weaken an oracle.

Alternatives, risks and unresolved decisions

Decision
Considered alternatives
Proposed choice / remaining uncertainty

Runtime improvement
Stay on patched April runtime; coherent latest-backend experiment; wholesale ClawX/AionUi migration
Preserve April as baseline and compare a bounded upgrade. Latest is a candidate, not a guaranteed fix. Avoid broad migration until a measured gap requires it.

Document architecture
Existing bundled adapters; upstream PDF analysis; external Office/conversion skill
Keep deterministic existing tools; compare upstream PDF for a specific fidelity gap; adopt an external runtime only if value exceeds install/package burden.

Email transport
Existing browser; existing Graph adapter; community Graph skill; classic-Outlook COM
Browser acceptance first, Graph coverage assessment next. Tenant authorization and source provenance remain real dependencies.

Compute
Bigger VM; fewer overlapping tasks; reduce preparation/IO/tool work
Measure saturation first. More CPU cannot authenticate Outlook or remove a lifecycle ownership defect. Existing VM shutdown/resize holds remain.

Skill reuse
Copy instructions/code; independently implement useful behavior; install catalogue wholesale
Prefer small, tested behavior changes. Resolve per-file provenance/license before copying; catalogue license is not every skill's license.

Distribution
Private expiring GCS; public release
Private diagnostic testing and GA remain distinct. No distribution during this pause; release notes must disclose provisioning and scope.

Open decisions are tracked, not guessed: supported Electron/Node tuple for the selected backend; live cloud model mapping and measured choice; remaining Graph parity/tenant grants; concrete latency budget; representative Windows 10/11 lane; P3 fidelity fix selection. None requires another blind build to document.

Concrete reuse decisions

- OpenClaw 2026.9.2 is an evaluation target, not an accepted upgrade; align Electron embedded Node, bundled Node, plugin SDK and Playwright, preserving failure oracles. Unreviewed worktree remains isolated.

- Cloud source intent is Gemini 2.5 Pro behind moe-demo-pro; final deployed model mapping and quality remain unproved. Compare a fixed corpus before changing model.

- Existing local document tools remain baseline. Add complete PDF obligation coverage and Excel type/formula/ID readback; do not install Python-based catalogue skills. Several mirror licenses are unresolved/proprietary.

- Existing Outlook Graph adapter should be assessed before adding another integration. Chrome readiness is separate from tenant sign-in. Graph send acceptance is not delivery. COM/OOM is classic-Outlook-only; advertised COM skill source provenance unresolved.

- AionUi contributes startup diagnostic and bounded-file patterns; OfficeCLI contributes inspect/edit/preview/readback contracts but is an additional .NET self-contained binary requiring future package/fidelity proof.

- whisper.cpp future ASR uses an on-demand CPU helper, quantized model comparison, one job/model, bounded audio and cleanup. No ASR implementation or measured memory/accuracy gain.

Historical objective and acceptance retained below

OBJECTIVE: Ship ClawX (Ministry of Education fork) to GA for T&T primary-school principals — an on-device-default desktop assistant that survived Raj's 2026-07-21 Ministry test (scored 0/5) and now provably passes it, installs unattended on Windows, works offline, and scales without a fleet-wide outage.
Source of truth is this board, not any doc. Ceiling for the agent is Ready; only a human moves a card to Done.
KEY RESULTS (each falsifiable):

- KR1 — Doc-tooling passes Raj's suite in-app. All 5 Ministry prompts (P1 .docx read, P2 .docx write, P3 .pdf, P4 .xlsx, P5 image) + folder discovery pass in a live, LLM-driven, in-app run on Windows; the model selects document.* every time and reaches for a Python skill zero times. Evidence = in-app transcript + tool-call trace. Status: capability GREEN, in-app run RED (outstanding).
- KR2 — Windows installer unattended on a clean VM. moe.11 installs on a clean Windows VM with a complete file tree; Gateway (18789) + host-API (13210) bind; 0 manual dependency steps. Evidence = install exit 0 + port probe + sha256. Status: GREEN on IAP lane (assisted/silent).
- KR3 — On-device default works with the network cut. On-device model answers a question grounded in a local document offline; local document path makes zero non-loopback egress. Evidence = eval lane G. Status: GREEN.
- KR4 — A cloud turn degrades instead of going silent. When a cloud turn cannot reach the provider, it fails over to on-device; the principal's Online/On-device toggle is not rewritten. Evidence = 25 unit tests + lane G G-degrade-classify. Status: GREEN (2026-08-21).
- KR5 — Offline records are durable. A form submission / audit record created offline is queued locally and delivered exactly once when the link returns. Evidence = lane G outbox checks. Status: RED — not built.
- KR6 — Cloud economics survive the fleet. Per-turn floor trimmed and per-user metering/caps in place so one heavy user cannot silently spend the fleet's month and trigger a fleet-wide 429. Evidence = trimmed floor measurement + broker accounting tests. Status: RED — trim on HOLD, caps not built.
- KR7 — A signed-in identity exists. Interactive Entra sign-in yields a stable oid; the app server stamps UserId from the authenticated session. Evidence = live sign-in producing a non-stub identifier. Status: RED — only a stub identity today; blocked on Ministry redirect URI.
- KR8 — The Ministry infra decisions are closed. Reply sent (owner gate) and a working session settles app-server hostname/reachability, the redirect URI, and prompt-caching. Evidence = sent reply + meeting notes. Status: DRAFT ready, owner-gated, UNSENT.
FACTS (in git/docs): Raj scored 0/5 on 2026-07-21 (commit 794cce74). Native document.* tools landed 2026-07-25 (98e805d8), 4 days after his test — he tested a build predating the fix. Steering + discovery fix landed 2026-08-20 (c1b18125); a 6-lane tool-selection eval was added the same day (73514b4f). moe.11 installs clean over the GCP IAP lane (verdict.md, sha256 b01bb6c3). Offline + degradation are built and tested (lane G, bde78d94).
INFERENCE (not yet proven end-to-end): Because the handler replay and the BM25 eval both bypass the live LLM, Raj's exact transcript could still reproduce in-app despite 7/7 handler pass. This is the one open GA risk on the doc-tooling line — a live in-app run is the only thing that closes it.
OPEN QUESTIONS (Ministry-owned): app-server hostname/reachability (internet-TLS vs iGovTT-only) — blocks the redirect URI; prompt-caching availability behind APIM; UserId = oid vs UPN; migration credential mechanism.

Also recommended: a dedicated service account for unattended IAP so token expiry stops killing this lane (design in VM_TEST_BASE.md).
- Slow-ready root cause found + fixed (contributing cause). On a fresh install the composer stayed disabled ~4–5 min while the gateway ready-fallback loop churned (retryAfterMs≈285000). Traced the boot chain: seedDefaultLocalProvider → runChannelPreflight → ensureBootableAgentsConfig → gateway start. Found a real gap in ensureBootableAgentsConfig: it short-circuited whenever an agents.defaults block existed at all — including an empty {} seeded by a prior boot when preflight resolved no model. A later boot that did resolve a valid modelRef then skipped writing it, leaving the gateway with a bootable-but-unbindable channel → composer disabled until the slow ready-fallback. Fix (commit 61be816e): only short-circuit when the block already carries a bindable model, or when there's no valid modelRef to upgrade with; otherwise write the model into the existing defaults in place. Idempotent. Added a regression test (empty→upgrade→stable); 4 boot-chain + 59 related config/router tests green, typecheck clean. Still owed for this card: the fresh clean-VM install recording (install → gateway ready → green on-device turn) on moe.12. This fix removes one persistent-empty-defaults contributor; the recording is the acceptance evidence. Leaving In Progress.

REOPENED FOR CURRENT OFFLINE EVIDENCE: earlier Ready scope does not establish current local-mode acceptance. Moe.22 ordinary on-device turns timed out. Direct native Ollama on the moe.25 VM answers a tiny no-tools request in 27.396s with context 32768 and 3.137 GiB allocation; this is not an app pass. Prior acceptance and dated history (scope retained)
The on-device model and local document tools must work with no connectivity — the 3:45pm daily-report deadline does not move when a school network drops.
FACTS: eval lane G runs the document path in a child process with fetch, net.Socket.prototype.connect, and DNS all patched to record+block every non-loopback target. Result: G-doc-read read 176 chars from a .docx with the network blocked; G-no-egress zero non-loopback attempts; G-model qwen2.5:3b-instruct answered from local file content in 2577ms (0.5–2.6s across runs); G-guard-live the guard caught all 6 deliberate egress attempts. On-device is already the default channel (electron/utils/store.ts:127).
INFERENCE: the guard is trustworthy because it is falsifiable — mutation-tested by reintroducing the net.connect packed-array bug, which turns the lane red while a naive G-no-egress still shows PASS. An inert guard is indistinguishable from a clean run, so the negative control is what makes the lane worth anything.
Accept (KR3): GREEN — met. Awaiting human close (agent ceiling is Ready).

### CLWX-29 — [CLWX-6] Cloud economics: trim per-turn floor + per-user metering/caps

- **State:** In Progress  |  **Priority:** none

100M tokens/month is exhausted at ~20 schools, and one principal bulk-processing a mailbox can spend the fleet's month with no warning — the first symptom is every principal getting 429 at once.
FACTS: a cloud turn costs ~10,650 tokens, ~71% of it a ~7,550-token fixed floor (31 tool defs ~3,594 + schemas ~1,918 + persona ~1,880) resent every turn. 100M/mo ≈ 2,635 cloud tasks for the whole fleet ≈ 0.6 tasks/principal/day at 200 schools; only ~6% of tasks can go to cloud. Branch fix/tool-catalog-trim (7add864b) cuts the floor toward ~2,000 for ~78% more capacity — it is on the pilot remote but under HOLD awaiting a reviewer pass, main untouched. No metering exists: the only rate-limit hit in the tree is an unused RATE_LIMITED enum (gateway/protocol.ts:77); model-broker authenticates but does not account.
INFERENCE: per-user caps cannot exist before identity (CLWX-7) does, since the cap key is the same as the UserId header. Counters live in PostgreSQL (no Redis) — negligible write volume at 200 principals.
OPEN QUESTIONS (Ministry): is prompt caching available behind APIM and are cached prefix tokens billed against the 100M? (free win if yes). Rollout curve / real fleet size.
Accept (KR6): per-turn floor measured <=2,500 tokens; model-broker meters per-user consumed-tokens, enforces per-user soft caps with a fleet reserve, and degrades a capped user to on-device rather than erroring; a fleet-level 429 degrades all clients (shares CLWX-4's mechanism).

**Comments (4):**

- New evidence that the held trim branch is no longer only an economics fix (moe.19 VM verify, 2026-09-06). No state change; the HOLD on fix/tool-catalog-trim @ 7add864b is untouched and not self-approved. This card holds the trim as leg 5a — cut the ~7,550-token per-turn floor for roughly 78% more fleet capacity. The moe.19 run surfaced a correctness reason for the same change, filed as CLWX-117: on the on-device channel the 3B model picks sessions_yield — session-control plumbing for a spawned sub-session — on a plain question. At top level there is no parent to yield to, so the turn parks with no terminal state at all: no answer, no error, no timeout. Evidence: skills/laptop/evidence/2026-09-06-moe19-verify/ondevice-stall-rootcause.md. Trimming session-control tools out of the catalog exposed to the on-device channel is the cheapest predicted fix for that stall, and it is work 7add864b already carries. So the branch now sits in front of two different outcomes: fleet capacity (this card) and a principal-visible hang (CLWX-117). What this comment does and does not do. It records the second reason and links the cards. It does not unhold the branch, push it, merge it, or treat the reviewer pass as done — that remains the owner's call, exactly as recorded on this card. If the trim stays held, CLWX-117's fix layers 2 (terminal turn watchdog, recommended into CLWX-47) and 3 (persona-level instruction) are the independent paths, and neither needs this branch. One honest caveat carried from the note: the trim is the predicted fix, not a proven one. Nobody has yet run the CLWX-117 prompt against a build with the trim applied. The falsifiability is written on that card.
- 5b cap logic landed (commit deff5c7d): services/model-broker/usage-meter.mjs + flag-gated wiring in server.mjs + tests/unit/model-broker-caps.test.ts (28 new tests across KR5/KR6, all green in the 1225-test full run; typecheck 0). Per-user daily soft cap + 90% fleet-reserve rule → structured 429 the KR4 client degrade path consumes; UserId header identity with anonymous fallback until KR7; flag OFF = byte-for-byte pre-caps behavior. Remaining for Ready: (a) 5a trim merge — owner-gated HOLD on fix/tool-catalog-trim; (b) fleet verification — gated on KR7 real UserId + real APIM hostname (reply now SENT, awaiting Raj).

### CLWX-30 — [CLWX-7] Real Entra sign-in + stable UserId identity

- **State:** In Progress  |  **Priority:** none

The app has no signed-in-user identity today, which blocks both the mandatory UserId header (Ministry §3.3) and per-user caps.
FACTS: the only userPrincipalName in the tree is the stub 'principal@school.example' (microsoft-graph/manager.ts:257). The live Outlook path identifies the principal only implicitly, by riding whichever account is signed into their Chrome session over CDP — deliberate and the only thing Conditional Access permits, but the app never learns the identity in a header-able form.
INFERENCE / design: backend-for-frontend — desktop does the interactive sign-in, the app server holds the client secret + tokens and stamps UserId from the authenticated session (never client-supplied). This also resolves the client-secret objection (a public/PKCE client is no longer needed once a confidential app server exists). Recommend oid over UPN (opaque, survives renames).
OPEN QUESTIONS (Ministry-gated): redirect URI — becomes https://<app-server-host>/auth/callback, so it depends on the hostname decision Raj has been asking about since 2026-07-20. UserId = oid vs UPN. Refresh-token lifetimes (longer = fewer forced-online re-auths).
Accept (KR7): a live interactive Entra sign-in produces a stable non-stub oid; an authenticated request to the app server carries a UserId stamped server-side, verified in App Insights.

**Comments (4):**

- 2026-09-03 completion tick: KR7 agent-buildable half DONE. UserId=oid stamped on the cloud gateway provider at seed AND re-stamped live on Graph sign-in/out (gateway reload threading was missing — caught by adversarial review, fixed + regression-tested). Broker now forwards sanitized UserId upstream for APIM/App-Insights attribution; the full seam (provider headers to openclaw.json to outbound call) is unit-tested. Client-stamped header is trust-limited (pilot-acceptable, per SCALE_ANALYSIS). Remaining half is Ministry-gated: App-Insights per-user verification needs KR8 real values. When values arrive this is a config swap + verification, not a build.
