### CLWX-71 — [feature/mcp] Thin MCP adapter over host-API :13210 - gated forms/outlook tools for any MCP client

- **State:** In Progress  |  **Priority:** medium

Area: integration   Priority: medium (post-GA enhancement; owner-requested research 2026-09-03)

Research verdict (docs/MCP_INTEGRATION_RESEARCH_2026-09-03.md, web-verified 2026-09-02/03)
NO MCP server anywhere can submit Microsoft Forms responses - no public Graph/REST API exists; the Power Automate connector remains read-only (doc upd. 2025-10-06). Browser MCPs (Playwright MCP --cdp-endpoint, chrome-devtools-mcp --browserUrl) CAN attach to the user Chrome at :18792 (Conditional Access constraint satisfied) but are UNGATED click/type surfaces - dev/debug only, never principal-facing.

Build
A ~200-line stdio MCP server (@modelcontextprotocol/sdk v2) that proxies the existing host-API endpoints as MCP tools: forms list/preview-daily-report/submit-daily-report/preview-suspension/submit-suspension + outlook open/read-inbox/draft/send (~9 registerTool blocks). The hard-confirm gates (confirm:true + title/subject fingerprint) stay server-side in FormsBrowserManager/outlook manager, so EVERY MCP client (Claude Code, Claude Desktop, future agents) inherits them and cannot bypass them. This is how MCP re-enhances the app: its capabilities become tools other agents can drive - same gates, same logs, zero new attack surface, no CDP contention.

Acceptance (QA + security bar)
1. Adapter connects from Claude Code; tools list correctly.
2. Read-only proof: forms preview + outlook read-inbox through MCP.
3. GATE PROOF through MCP: submit/send WITHOUT confirm is refused end-to-end; with confirm:true + matching fingerprint it completes on the test.fac sandbox only.
4. :13210 auth token via env var (never argv/logs); adapter logs counts only, never payload bodies.
5. MCP SDK in dependencies, not devDependencies (dependency-class-auditor rule).
6. README note: raw browser MCPs are dev/debug only.

Non-goals
No Graph MCP for forms (no API to wrap). Logic Apps-as-MCP-server (preview) revisits when Ministry IT stands up the Power Automate path - the sanctioned long-term fix is writing the form's backing store directly, exposed as a gated server-side tool.

Sources
microsoft/mcp catalog; microsoft/playwright-mcp; ChromeDevTools/chrome-devtools-mcp; softeria/ms-365-mcp-server; learn.microsoft.com Forms connector + Logic Apps MCP preview + Enterprise Graph MCP; modelcontextprotocol/typescript-sdk. Full report in docs/MCP_INTEGRATION_RESEARCH_2026-09-03.md.

**Comments (1):**

- Adapter BUILT + live-proven + review-hardened (2026-09-06 driver tick; commits 0d8b0fbb + 1e3a9170 + 61fb895e) — and its review lane found a PRODUCTION two-gate hole. Card Todo → In Progress. - Built: scripts/clawx-mcp-server.mjs — stdio MCP server (SDK low-level API, no phantom deps), nine tools proxying the host-API verbatim so the two-gate send, forms hard-confirm, kill-switch and audit-first outbox writes ALL stay server-side; token env-only with fail-fast; stderr-only logging with identifier-shaped key names + counts; pnpm mcp:serve; docs/MCP_ADAPTER.md incl. the raw-browser-MCPs-are-dev/debug-only warning (acceptance 6); SDK in dependencies (acceptance 5). - Live proof (scripts/clwx71-mcp-handshake.ts, SDK client over stdio vs the RUNNING app): handshake OK; tools/list 9/9 exact-set (acceptance 1); outlook_send_email WITHOUT confirm → status "refused" end-to-end with the exact confirm-gate reason (acceptance 3 refusal leg). Recorded honestly: the installed app is moe.10 so /api/forms/* 404s (relayed readably; provable next build); outlook_read_inbox returns the readable Chrome-attach error (proxy path proven; live read Chrome-gated). - THE FIND (Codex HIGH, confirmed in source, fixed in production code 1e3a9170): sendEmail's current-reviewed branch DROPPED the subject — a bare {confirm:true} (or confirm + wrong subject) sent whatever single draft was open with ZERO second-gate verification, reachable from the in-app agent identically. ### CLWX-77 — [testing] Artifact-grade corner-gap matrix: every doc type x every command against the PACKAGED runtime

- **State:** In Progress  |  **Priority:** high

CLWX-HANDOFF-CURRENT-20260908
SOURCE DELTA: reviewed PDF source-excerpt repair 7a61a964 preserves explicit deadlines/submission instructions and the raw extraction contract. Source/parser checks pass; ordinary installed P3 answer coverage remains unproved. Test owner must run D0 plus all five principal prompts, complete obligation recall, typed spreadsheet values, generated-file reopen and required format/command matrix on one artifact. CLWX-115 retains its distinct meal/shirt association fixture.
Current plan: docs/COMPLETION_PLAN.md. Evidence: docs/evidence/WINDOWS_PLAN_EXECUTION_2026-09-08.md. Research: docs/research/OPENCLAW_WINDOWS_IMPROVEMENT_STUDY_2026-09-08.md. GA RED; source/build work paused for the next-agent handoff.
Prior acceptance and dated history (scope retained)
Why (owner ask: "look around the corner")
Every packaging-class defect so far (playwright-core moe.9, canvas binding moe.15) shipped because our tests run from the repo workspace, which resolves repo node_modules and masks packaged-runtime gaps. The systemic fix is a suite that exercises the ARTIFACT: the bundled gateway + packaged node, per doc type and per command.

Scope - doc types to cover
pdf (text + SCANNED/image-only + large >10MB + password-protected -> readable refusal), docx (+ legacy .doc -> readable refusal), xlsx (+ csv), pptx (status ambiguity noted in the audit - support or refuse cleanly), images (png/jpg via VLM path), odt/rtf -> refusal wording. Each: read, summarise-turn, and where applicable write.

Scope - commands
document.read_* / write_* via a real gateway process from build/openclaw (not repo imports); outlook + forms tool registration smoke; skill-load smoke for the principal bundle. Optionally driven through the CLWX-71 MCP adapter once it exists - an MCP client against the packaged app is exactly the artifact-grade transport.

Acceptance
1. New harness target (pnpm harness:artifact) spawning the bundled runtime; one row per doc-type x command with PASS/REFUSED-READABLY/FAIL.
2. Wired into preflight for package:win/mac (can be a fast subset) + full matrix in CI/nightly.
3. Unknown/unsupported types produce principal-readable refusals, never raw stack traces (principal-proxy bar).
4. First full run documented with gaps filed as cards.

Source
Owner directive 2026-09-03 + CLWX-72 lesson: test the artifact, not the workspace.

### CLWX-95 — [hardening/chat] Degraded turn orphans at prompt.submitted — on-device answer never lands after gateway-restart switch

- **State:** In Progress  |  **Priority:** none

Observed on the moe.17 verify addendum (skills/laptop/evidence/2026-09-03-moe17-verify/RESULT.md): the cloud→on-device channel switch is applied via a gateway RESTART; the interrupted turn's trajectory stops at prompt.submitted with no model response ever produced, and the renderer spins "still-thinking" for the full 420s (answerText=null). The degrade notice + switch + preference-preservation all work (CLWX-78 core PASS); the gap is that the degraded turn itself never completes.
Acceptance: after a mid-turn channel degrade, the interrupted turn is either resumed on the new channel exactly once or terminated with a visible, anonymised outcome — never an indefinite spinner. Verdict-tension note: moe.17 PASSed under the "switch + notice + attempt" bar; this card is the stricter "answer must land or fail visibly" bar, explicitly owner-arbitrated.

The behaviour is correct — but nothing pinned it. The send-time degrade route deliberately passes { skipGatewayRefresh: true } (settings.ts:129) because it runs inside a failed turn the renderer resends at once, and on Windows a refresh becomes a restart that loses the port race. Boot preflight is the opposite case: nothing runs after it to refresh the gateway, so suppressing there would leave the four stores correct on disk while the live gateway kept serving the previous model — the exact silence-on-send shape this card closed. A refactor copying the degrade call site into the boot path would have shipped green. - Root cause found, fixed in-tree, and the "never an indefinite spinner" half of the acceptance is now closed at the store layer &mdash; state NOT moved (two review lanes still open + next-build live probe). Commits ddb1aa30 &rarr; c22e7a26 &rarr; 55b488db &rarr; a4282f1a. 1. Why the degraded turn orphaned (the restart-vs-per-run seam this card tracks). The four-store config write is NOT a runtime cutover. The bundled gateway resolves every turn from a config snapshot pinned at boot (loadPinnedRuntimeConfig; its clearConfigCache is a no-op); external edits are picked up only by a watcher that debounces ~500ms, batches, and can be switched off entirely (gateway.reload.mode: "off"). So the old degrade path had exactly two outcomes: bounce the gateway mid-turn (the orphan reproduced live on moe.18/moe.19 &mdash; send races the reload, messagesBefore:0 messagesAfter:0) or skip the bounce and resend onto the provider that just failed while reporting success. Neither can land an answer. 2. What replaced it. A session-level model pin over sessions.patch: the gateway re-reads session overrides from disk on every turn and they outrank both the agent and the config default, so it is immune to the snapshot and the watcher, and it does not restart the runtime (safe to use inside a failed turn, unlike a reload). The cutover is acknowledged &mdash; isSessionModelCutoverConfirmed() compares the gateway's resolved readback against the requested ref and fails closed: an RPC that merely did not throw is not evidence. Consequences wired: an unproven cutover means NO resend and honest copy ("could not switch"), never a claimed switch; a proven cutover resends the turn exactly once; an explicit channel pick clears the pin; and a pin left on disk by an earlier run is reconciled once per session per app run, only against a proven Online default (a pin survives quitting the app and the boot preflight cannot see it, so without this a school that lost the network on Monday answers on-device all week with the composer still reading Online). 3. Trust lens (principal-proxy, separate lane) &mdash; verdict FAIL, both findings closed same tick in a4282f1a. HIGH: the recovery was silent. The config transaction and the acknowledged cutover (15s budget) were both awaited inside the failed turn with nothing on screen changing &mdash; at 3:40pm against the 3:45pm daily-report deadline that reads as a hung app, and principals retry into it. Fix: the degrade notice now goes up BEFORE those awaits with a spinner and "switching to the model on this device &mdash; this takes a few seconds, you don't need to do anything", not dismissible while running, and replaced by the outcome (switched / answered here / could-not-switch) or cleared if the failover itself fails. Copy precedence moved to a pure module (src/lib/degrade-notice.ts) so in-progress outranking every terminal state is unit-provable without rendering. MEDIUM (red banner stacked under the amber notice): closed by the existing errorBannerVisibility rule &mdash; a notice that is not claiming success suppresses same-class transport banners &mdash; now pinned by a regression row so the new state cannot silently fall out of it. Self-inflicted hole found while fixing and closed in the same commit: the host-API transport has NO timeout of its own (neither the IPC proxy nor the browser fallback), so a wedged main process would have left a non-dismissible spinner up forever &mdash; worse than the frozen error. The route is now bounded by DEGRADE_ROUTE_TIMEOUT_MS and expiry takes the failure path (no notice, real error visible, nothing resent). 4. Codex cross-model lane: 3/3 findings triaged, verdict captured verbatim at docs/evidence/CODEX_ADVERSARIAL_REVIEW_2026-09-06_CLWX-95.md (it is what surfaced the config-write-is-not-a-cutover claim in the first place). Claude lanes: trust complete (above); code-review and refutation lenses were still running at this comment &mdash; per the review bar a Codex PASS never overrides a Claude FAIL, so the card does not move until both land and any finding is confirmed-and-fixed or refuted with evidence. 5. Falsifiability (each mutation applied, run, reverted). Drop the early notice set() &rarr; exactly the 2 ordering rows fail, the other 36 pass. Disable the in-progress copy branch &rarr; 3 precedence rows fail. Remove the route timeout &rarr; the strand row fails. Earlier in the chain: harness honesty proofs in c22e7a26 (a chip is not an answer, a pill is not a runtime) and the K10 false-ANSWERED shape (prompt echo counted as a reply). Gates: pnpm typecheck clean; full unit suite 186 files / 1686 passed, 6 skipped; degrade + copy + error-display suites 64 passed; eslint clean on every touched file. The anonymised-identity hard rule is asserted directly &mdash; no model id, vendor name, or cost string can appear in any notice wording. Honest limits (why this is not Ready). (a) NEXT-BUILD GATE: everything above is in-tree; the installed moe.19 binary on the VM still carries the old path, so the live "cloud stalls &rarr; on-device answer lands in-place" proof needs a new Windows build plus a VM run. That lane is parked on an expired credential &mdash; owner action gcloud auth login (the verify rig now classifies this correctly as BLOCKED instead of prescribing a billable VM start, 30f97164). (b) Residual coverage carried openly from the Codex lane: end-to-end through the real /api/settings/preferredChannel route with a runtime readback, rather than the store-level mock used here. (c) The sibling recovery wedge (CLWX-96) still depends on the same seam; commented there separately.

- Orphan REPRODUCED LIVE on moe.18 (2026-09-06 VM verify) &mdash; and scoped to the gateway-restart-during-send window. When the driver switched channel mid-turn (cloud&rarr;on-device) with the on-device model down, the switch triggers a gateway RELOAD (log: Scheduling Gateway reload after provider switch to "ollama-ollamalo" &rarr; Starting Gateway process ~2s later) and the send RACED the reload: verdict NO_RESPONSE, messagesBefore:0 messagesAfter:0 &mdash; the user turn never produced an assistant response and no error/degrade notice fired for it (exactly the prompt.submitted orphan this card describes). Contrast: a CLEAN on-device outage with NO mid-turn switch (app already on-device) does NOT orphan &mdash; it raises the correct anonymised degrade prompt at ~23s (see CLWX-78 comment). ### CLWX-96 — [hardening/chat] Post-degrade recovery wedge: stale offline banner + silent sends until full app relaunch

- **State:** In Progress  |  **Priority:** none

Observed on the moe.17 verify addendum (skills/laptop/evidence/2026-09-03-moe17-verify/RESULT.md): after hosts-restore following a degrade, the post-restore Online turn returned NO_RESPONSE with messagesAfter=0 (silent send) while a stale "offline" degrade banner was still displayed; Online only worked again after a full app relaunch. Contradicts the intended "Online resumes automatically once available" behaviour.
Acceptance: after connectivity returns, the next Online turn succeeds without an app restart; the degrade banner clears when the provider probe succeeds; a silent send (accepted composer input, no turn, no error) is treated as a defect class of its own with a watchdog. Trust lens: this is exactly the 3:30pm-dropped-connection scenario the principal-proxy vetoes on.

**Comments (6):**

Latency was 99,184 ms, so this is functional proof, not latency acceptance. The subsequent cancel-next check also proved the next Online send answered and settled after an observable stop. Evidence: installed-acceptance-34171848832/{online-content-review.json,online-transcript-review.json,cancel-next-20260908T0158Z/cancel-and-next-summary.json}. Scope boundary: this does not prove the stale offline-banner clause, provider probe clears, silent-send watchdog, or post-degrade recovery after a controlled cloud fault. Keep this card In Progress.

- Shared root cause with CLWX-95 identified; two of the three acceptance clauses now have code behind them, the third (banner clears on a successful probe) and the silent-send watchdog are still unbuilt. Staying in Backlog. Why the moe.17 post-restore turn was silent. The mechanism is the seam CLWX-95 traced: the bundled gateway resolves every turn from a config snapshot pinned at boot, so a channel change that only writes config either does nothing to the running turn or forces a runtime reload &mdash; and a send that races that reload is orphaned (messagesBefore:0 messagesAfter:0, reproduced live on moe.18/moe.19). That is the same shape as this card's NO_RESPONSE with a composer that accepted the input: not a lost message, a turn that was never resolved by anything. What landed in ddb1aa30/c22e7a26/55b488db/a4282f1a that bears directly on this card: - Runtime channel is read from the runtime, not the preference. The failover decision now classifies the account the channel transaction marked default (mirroring the main process's own getActiveChannel) instead of deriving from preferredChannel. A preference that still reads Online over an already-degraded runtime used to be mis-read as a cloud turn and degraded a second time &mdash; the exact confusion this card describes. - The stuck-on-device half of clause 1. A session model pin survives quitting the app and the boot preflight cannot see it, so a school that dropped connectivity on Monday would keep answering on-device all week with the composer reading Online. There is now a once-per-session-per-run reconcile that clears such a pin, but ONLY against an Online default that has been proven reachable, plus an explicit-channel-pick path that clears the pin immediately. That covers "the principal picks Online again and it works" without a restart. - Stale success-claiming banner cannot outlive a newer failure. A notice claiming "resent on this device" is dropped when a newer terminal error arrives (moe.18 D1, also a Codex-lane finding: a failed on-device resend was invisible behind the stale notice). The in-progress notice is bounded too, so no notice can be left spinning by a wedged host API. Still open on this card &mdash; do not read the above as closed: - Clause 2 (banner clears when the provider probe succeeds) is NOT built. Today the notice clears on a newer failure, on an explicit dismiss, or on a channel change. Nothing polls the cloud provider and retracts the notice on its own, so an amber "on this device" notice can still outlive the outage until the principal acts. That is the remaining build here. - Clause 3 (silent send as its own defect class, with a watchdog) is NOT built. CLWX-95 removed one cause of orphaned turns on the degrade path; it did not add a detector, so any other cause is still invisible. The natural home is the slow-turn watchdog on CLWX-47 (a turn with no stream and no error past a threshold is a silent send, not a slow one) &mdash; recommend building both in that card rather than a third timer. - No live re-proof. Everything above is in-tree; the installed moe.19 binary still carries the old path. The hosts-restore replay from the moe.17 addendum needs a new Windows build on the VM, and that lane is parked on an expired credential (owner action gcloud auth login).
- Related to the CLWX-95 seam (2b21d2a8) - NOT moving. The run-ownership token and terminal-error lastSentPayload handling landed for CLWX-94 reduce the orphan/replay surface, but the post-degrade recovery wedge (stale offline banner + silent sends until full relaunch) depends on the same restart-vs-per-run refresh decision tracked in CLWX-95. Kept open pending that wiring + live probe.

This is exactly the fail-closed case CLWX-106 exists for: static/source progress plus partial installed evidence must not become GA.
- Scope sharpened and half the mechanism now landed, 2026-09-07 (round 2). The piece that was missing when this card was written is now in place: the gate's skip/fail decision is testable. It lived inline in scripts/ga-gate.mjs, which cannot be imported (it spawns pnpm and calls process.exit at module scope), so nothing could contradict it — and that is exactly how a fail-open shipped inside the gate and survived two review lenses. The judgement now lives in scripts/ga-gate-verdict.mjs as pure functions with no I/O and no import side effects, imported by the gate as its single decision point, pinned by 17 rows in tests/unit/ga-gate-verdict.test.ts with 6/6 mutation legs REAL. What this card still owns, unchanged: the exit code. process.exit(fails.length === 0 ? 0 : 1) is still the whole rule, so a run whose required tier was fully BLOCKED still exits 0 while printing the honest INCOMPLETE verdict. The refusal to change that today stands and the reason is recorded: this card owns skip-fail semantics and release enforcement together, and splitting them yields a gate that is RED on every dev machine without a live Outlook tab, which trains people to ignore RED. Two additions to acceptance from this round: - A row must declare what it requires. The classifier is now fail-closed — only a row that explicitly declared the lane contract and exited exactly 2 may be BLOCKED, every other non-zero is a product FAIL. That is a floor, not the goal. The goal remains a requires field per row so the gate can compute coverage instead of being told it. - The operator-asked case. GA_GATE_SEND=1 with no authed tab must be a BLOCKED non-zero, because the operator explicitly asked for that proof and did not get it. Today it skips quietly inside a GREEN. One over-claim corrected in place: the header comment claiming "non-zero if a REQUIRED tier was fully skipped" had no implementation behind it at all and has been rewritten to state the truth and point here. Proven by a static run printing [T1] live-lane ... SKIP beside GATE GREEN. And the standing reason this card matters more than its Backlog state suggests: every GREEN gate report on disk — 09-03, 09-04, 09-05, 09-06 — carries live-lane ... SKIP ... GA_GATE_STATIC=1. Four green scorecards, zero live email rows executed in any of them. Under the current exit rule that is indistinguishable from four green runs that did test email. That is the false-green this card exists to make structurally impossible.
- ga-sprint-driver tick 14 — this card's scope is now sharper, and one thing it was carrying has been split off. No state change; still Backlog, still the owner's call to schedule. Confirmed, with a reproduction: the gate's exit contract has always over-claimed. The header said "non-zero if any non-skipped check fails, or if a REQUIRED tier was fully skipped", but the exit logic is only process.exit(fails.length === 0 ? 0 : 1) — no skip rule exists anywhere in the file. Proof that this predates any change made this tick: a static run printed [T1] live-lane ... SKIP (GA_GATE_STATIC=1) alongside GATE GREEN — 7 pass, 0 fail, 3 skip. A required tier skipped in full, exit 0. Done this tick (labelling only, deliberately not enforcement): the verdict now prints / PARTIAL — N required check(s) were BLOCKED and never ran (...) when a required-tier row was blocked by the environment, in both the console and docs/evidence/GA_GATE_<date>.md; and the header comment now states the real exit behaviour and points here instead of describing a rule that does not exist. Flag-driven skips are excluded from the qualifier on purpose — one that fires on every default run is warning fatigue, not signal. Why the exit code was NOT changed here. Two reasons, both worth recording so this is not re-litigated. (a) Scope: this card owns skip-fail semantics and the release-path enforcement together, and splitting them risks a gate that is RED on every developer machine without a live Outlook tab while the release path still does not check freshness — which trains people to ignore RED, the same failure the qualifier avoids. (b) It is a contract change to THE acceptance test (CLWX-90, urgent/Ready) and belongs in a scheduled slice, not a side effect of a probe fix. Acceptance refinement, from the cross-model adversarial lens this tick (it independently reached the same finding and pushed for the exit change): track required checks explicitly rather than inferring them from tier membership, and include an explicitly-requested proof in that set — i.e. GA_GATE_SEND=1 with no usable Outlook tab must be a BLOCKED non-zero, not a quiet skip, because the operator asked for that proof by setting the flag. Intentional exclusions (GA_GATE_STATIC=1, and opt-in tiers left unset) must stay exit-0. The blocked flag now carried on every skip row in scripts/ga-gate.mjs is the hook to build this on — the classification work is already done, only the exit rule and the release-path check remain.

### CLWX-117 — On-device turn parks forever on a top-level sessions_yield (no terminal state)

- **State:** In Progress  |  **Priority:** high

CLWX-HANDOFF-CURRENT-20260908
ON-DEVICE DEFECT STILL OPEN: native-route/context source repair 0b46e833 is reviewed/integrated in frozen f93ac8b3; full app prompt and offline document behavior are NOT_RUN. Direct native diagnostic confirms API/context feasibility only. Held test repair 24e1cfd3 fails with actual OpenClaw 2026.9.2: canvas policy family expands to show_widget while old fixture supplies literal canvas. Next executor must bind the oracle to the actual tool catalog and dependency version, obtain independent review, then verify bounded terminal/cancel/next-turn behavior in the installed app. CLWX-126 owns engine selection research.
Current plan: docs/COMPLETION_PLAN.md. Evidence: docs/evidence/WINDOWS_PLAN_EXECUTION_2026-09-08.md. Research: docs/research/OPENCLAW_WINDOWS_IMPROVEMENT_STUDY_2026-09-08.md. GA RED; source/build work paused for the next-agent handoff.
Prior acceptance and dated history (scope retained)
Found live on the moe.19 Windows VM verify, 2026-09-06. Root-cause note: skills/laptop/evidence/2026-09-06-moe19-verify/ondevice-stall-rootcause.md; run scorecard: RESULT.md in the same folder (row "On-device turn: top-level sessions_yield — RED, new defect").
What the principal sees. An assistant bubble appears, one execution step reads sessions_yield {}, and no answer text ever renders. The turn never terminates: no error, no timeout, no "something went wrong". A bubble that never fills. Indefinite, not slow — which is what makes this different from the latency class.
Observed (driver JSON, fresh session). freshSession: true, messagesBefore: 0, messagesAfter: 2, executionSteps: ["sessions_yield {}"], degradeNoticeSeen: false, runErrorSeen: false, answerText: null, settled: false, verdict TIMED_OUT_MID_TURN at the 300s budget.
The usual suspects are all ruled out. Channel on-device with the four stores coherent (set through the composer pill, CHANNEL_BEFORE online → CHANNEL_AFTER on-device). Gateway stable, no bounce during the turn. Session provably empty before the send. And the model is not the bottleneck: a direct POST /api/generate against the same guest ollama and the same qwen2.5:3b-instruct answered in 8.2s wall (0.1s of it decode), and the ollama process held only 58 CPU-seconds of lifetime CPU — nowhere near 300s of generation on 4 vCPUs. The turn is stalled in the agent/tool loop, not waiting on tokens.
Root cause. sessions_yield is session-control plumbing for a spawned sub-session: it hands control back to a parent. The renderer models it exactly that way — src/pages/Chat/task-visualization.ts:147 treats a sessions_yield step as the closer of the active branch. At top level there is no branch and no parent to yield to. The 3B on-device model picks the tool anyway on a plain question, and the turn parks forever.
This is the non-self-recovering variant of the known on-device tool-cascade class. The previously observed residual was a spurious exec, which self-recovers and was therefore triaged cosmetic. sessions_yield does not self-recover — the turn has no terminal state at all.
Why our own harness hid it (the more important half). windows-pilot/scripts/pilot-electron-cdp-probe.js:342 already prompts around this defect: "Use only direct tools in this session; do not use sessions_spawn, sessions_yield, subagents, or background sessions." That instruction lives only in a test probe — not in the product persona or system prompt. So a principal typing an ordinary question is unprotected, and every eval run through that probe was steering the model away from the defect instead of measuring it. The turn driver used on this run carries no such instruction, which is exactly why the defect surfaced. Same shape as the skipGatewayRefresh dead-code lesson: a harness that prompts around a defect reports GREEN over a broken product.
Fix layers, cheapest first (predicted, none executed).

- Trim session-control tools (sessions_spawn, sessions_yield, subagent tools) out of the catalog exposed to the on-device channel. Branch fix/tool-catalog-trim @ 7add864b already carries this work and is under owner HOLD (see CLWX-29) — not unheld by this card.
- Terminal turn watchdog so no tool-loop stall can render as an infinite spinner; the principal gets a plain "I couldn't finish that". Natural home is CLWX-47 (slow-turn watchdog) rather than a third timer — commented there.
- Persona-level instruction mirroring the probe's, as defence in depth.
Acceptance. (a) A fresh-session no-tool prompt on the on-device channel produces answer text, and sessions_yield never appears at top level. (b) Falsifiability: with the fix reverted, the same prompt must again produce sessions_yield with no answer. (c) No turn can end without a terminal state — a stalled tool loop surfaces a plain refusal, never an unbounded spinner. (d) The steering instruction that hid this is removed from the probe or mirrored into the product, so no eval can score GREEN by prompting around it.
Agent-filed at Backlog from the moe.19 verify. Scope, priority, and whether to unhold 7add864b are the owner's call.

### CLWX-120 — Eval row W3.2 counts a persistent safe refusal as a product FAIL while its sibling W8.4 skips honestly

- **State:** In Progress  |  **Priority:** medium

In the 2026-09-07 round-2 gate run, outlook-eval went RED on W3.2 with status=not_found attachments=undefined. The per-check log shows the real cause: reading-pane settle FAILED: pane stayed on a different message — i.e. the CLWX-46 stale-read guard doing its job and refusing to report a message it could not confirm was the right one.
That refusal is the SAFE behaviour. The dedicated guard row for exactly this (stale-read check (CLWX-46 guard)) PASSED in the same run. So the suite has two rows observing the same mechanism and grading it in opposite directions.
Sibling precedent inside the same file: W8.4 skips honestly when its precondition is absent instead of scoring a FAIL. W3.2 does not, so a healthy safe-refusal is charged to the product and the gate's headline verdict flips to RED for a non-defect.
Not a request to loosen the guard. The guard stays; the grading is what is wrong. Options: (a) W3.2 declares its precondition (a settleable reading pane on the seeded message) and SKIPs when it is unmet, exactly as W8.4 does; or (b) W3.2 asserts the refusal itself as the expected outcome, which turns it into a second positive test of CLWX-46 rather than a flaky read test.
Interacts with the eval-determinism card: W3.2 is one of the two rows that swap between runs, so fixing the grading may also remove half the observed flake. Fix the grading first and re-measure before attributing the rest of the flake elsewhere.

**Comments (1):**

- FIXED 2026-09-07 — the root cause was not the grader. Two different situations were arriving at the grader as the same value, so no grader could have been correct. The register framed this as "W3.2 grades a healthy safe refusal as a product FAIL", which is the visible symptom. Reading the chain (waitForReadingPaneSettle outlook-actions.ts:1771 &rarr; openMessageById :1671 &rarr; openMessageByIdInCurrentViewOrInbox :1617) showed something worse: openMessageById returned a bare boolean, and every distinct failure collapsed into one not_found. So the eval could not tell these apart, and neither could the agent, the UI, or a human reading a log: - not_in_list — the message could not be reached at all. Genuinely absent from the view; a real product failure. - stale_read_guard — the row WAS found and clicked, and the CLWX-46 guard then declined to confirm the reading pane had settled on it. The product refusing to hand over content it cannot attribute. It makes no claim about the message existing. Why this mattered beyond the eval, and the reason it was worth fixing properly rather than patching the row: the principal was being told "Could not locate message ..." about a message sitting visibly in their own inbox. That is a false statement produced by a safety feature working correctly — the worst combination for trust, because the honest behaviour looks like a bug and sends them hunting for mail that never moved. The fix is a typed discriminator, deliberately NOT a substring of the message text. Keying a verdict off log or message text is the exact fail-open closed last tick (GATE-VERDICT-FAIL-OPEN): any row can emit the token, so a consumer that greps for it eventually greps a lie. New MessageLocateFailure = 'not_in_list' | 'stale_read_guard' in electron/services/outlook-browser/types.ts, surfaced as an optional notFoundReason on every result that can carry not_found from this locator — read, reply, forward, markRead, listAttachments, downloadAttachment. One design choice worth recording, because it is the part that could have gone wrong silently. The wrapper now returns an object ({ outcome, reason? }) rather than a widened string union. A new union member would have slipped straight past all five located === 'not_found' checks and fallen through to the SUCCESS path — a new fail-open introduced by the fix for a fail-open. The object shape breaks typecheck at every call site until each is updated: the compiler produced exactly 10 errors across the 5 sites (2 each), which is how I know none was missed rather than believing my own grep. The same lie was hiding in the test harness. Existing rows stubbed openMessageById with true/false, and the TestActions cast still declared Promise<boolean> — so TypeScript could not flag them, and true !== 'opened' would have quietly routed those tests down the not_found path while still passing. Fixed the cast, then the stubs. A cast that lies about a private signature defeats exactly the compiler-enumeration the fix relies on. Grading change (the original ask), fail-closed. W3.2 in scripts/v2-eval.ts now reports UNEXERCISED only on positive evidence of notFoundReason === 'stale_read_guard'. An absent field falls through to FAIL, so a leaner transport that never sets it cannot buy a green; not_in_list still FAILs; and data returned-but-WRONG (missing filename/size/mime) is always a FAIL, never a skip. A skip requires a refusal — that asymmetry is what keeps the row from going soft, since "cannot fail" would make it a coverage claim the suite has not earned. Falsifiability — 3 mutation legs, all REAL, each failing exactly the row named for it: (A) restore the pre-fix conflation at the origin (settle=false &rarr; not_in_list) &rarr; 1 failed / 93 passed, fails the origin row; (B) fail-open precedence (report the FIRST attempt's reason so a transient stale pane masks genuine absence) &rarr; 1 failed / 93, fails the second-attempt row; (C) drop propagation through listAttachments &rarr; 1 failed / 93. Control 94/94 before AND after, source verified byte-identical. Note (A) exists because the other 6 rows stub openMessageById and therefore pin propagation, not origin — without it the conflation could have been restored inside that method with every other row still green. Agent-facing contract updated too (extensions/moe-principal-assistant/index.mjs): the outlook.read_email description now documents both reasons and instructs the assistant, on stale_read_guard, to say it could not confirm it had the right message open and is retrying — and never that the message is missing. The field reached the agent already (manager and host-API route both pass the result through verbatim, verified by reading them), but an undocumented field is one the model cannot use. Gates: typecheck 0; lint 0 errors (52 pre-existing warnings, count unchanged); full unit suite 1741 passed / 6 skipped across 188 files (was 1734 — +7 new rows). Named residual, not a claim of completeness: the W3.2 grading logic itself has no unit row — v2-eval.ts is a live script with no importable seam, so the guard against a future fail-open THERE is a comment, not a test. That is the same gap that let a fail-open ship inside ga-gate two ticks ago, and the same remedy would apply (extract the judgement to a pure module). Recorded rather than quietly accepted. Ceiling note: this card is NOT moved to Ready. It changed code, and per the driver's separate-lane rule the session that wrote a change never approves it. The review lane is the next step.

### CLWX-123 — draft_email refuses a draft its own fill path just committed - recipient assertion mismatch (reproduced 4/4 live)

- **State:** In Progress  |  **Priority:** high

CLWX-HANDOFF-CURRENT-20260908
DRAFT FIDELITY REMAINS OPEN: retain the original reproduced recipient-assertion mismatch and its compose/readback criteria. Graph draft-permission repair is a separate reviewed source change on CLWX-40; it does not prove browser recipient, subject or body fidelity. An authenticated user Chrome profile and exact installed candidate are required for reviewed draft/reopen testing. No current tenant acceptance or new dispatch is claimed.
Current plan: docs/COMPLETION_PLAN.md. Evidence: docs/evidence/WINDOWS_PLAN_EXECUTION_2026-09-08.md. Research: docs/research/OPENCLAW_WINDOWS_IMPROVEMENT_STUDY_2026-09-08.md. GA RED; source/build work paused for the next-agent handoff.
Prior acceptance and dated history (scope retained)
Symptom. The live Outlook eval's W4.1 row (draft_email opens compose pane with To/Subject/Body filled) fails on every run: status=failed leftOpen=true. Reproduced 4/4 on consecutive runs against outlook.cloud.microsoft on 2026-09-07. Not a flake — the same four runs were 16 pass / 1 fail / 1 skip of 18 with zero verdict flips (see CLWX-119).
The driver's own message, which the eval had been discarding:
Draft was not marked ready because the open draft does not contain all requested To recipients. Review or close any saved Outlook draft before retrying.That string is at electron/services/outlook-browser-v2/outlook-actions.ts:3790, inside describeRecipientAssertionMismatch (:3786). It fires when missingRecipientNeedles(asArray(args.to), snapshot.to).length > 0 or hasUnexpectedRecipient(...).
Why this is a real defect and not the guard working. The recipient in question was put there by our own code moments earlier — fillField(page, 'To', ...) at :503 followed by commitRecipientField(page, 'To', to) at :504. The fill path succeeds and then the read-back path does not recognise what the fill path wrote. So the write and read halves of the same driver disagree about the same field. A protective assertion that refuses correctly-filled drafts is a false negative on the Email objective's critical path: the principal is told to "review or close any saved Outlook draft" when there is nothing wrong with the draft and, at rest, no draft at all.
What has already been ruled out (each with evidence, so the next session does not repeat it):

- Not a wedged pre-existing draft. draftEmail emits this status=failed, draftLeftOpen=true pair at exactly one branch (:466, hasAnyVisibleOpenDraft true and recoverComposeState declining to clear), so the obvious hypothesis was a stale compose pane. A new read-only diagnostic, scripts/outlook-compose-state-probe.ts, mirrors the detector's selectors and reports at rest: subject inputs (visible): 0, rich-text boxes: 0, detector would report: hasCompose=false subject=ABSENT bodyEmpty=true hasDiscard=false. Nothing is wedged.
- Not CLWX-58 regressing. scripts/clwx58-compose-recovery-check.ts PASSES (exit 0, both legs OK). Compose recovery works; this is a case its guard does not cover.
- Not the eval's grading. That was the other half of today's work and is fixed and pinned (CLWX-119, commit 2005b13c). This row fails on the product's own reported status, not on how it is judged.
Two candidate mechanisms, both in the read-back path, not yet discriminated. The needle is an exact email string: expectedRecipientNeedles (:146) extracts the address and lowercases it, then missingRecipientNeedles (:207) requires it to be a substring of snapshot.to.join(' ').

- Person-chip display name. Outlook resolves a typed address into a recipient chip whose visible text may be the display name only. snapshot.to comes from splitRecipients(recipientFieldText(root, 'To')) (:4230), and recipientFieldText (:4144) returns the first non-empty valueText among elements matching [aria-label="To"], [aria-label*="To" i], [role="textbox"][aria-label*="To" i], [contenteditable="true"][aria-label*="To" i]. If the chip renders a display name, an email-string needle can never match it.
- Wrong element wins the selector race. [aria-label*="To" i] is a substring match and the compose To row typically has both a chip well and an inner combobox/input. If a differently-labelled element (or a container whose text includes extra chrome) is matched first and returns non-empty, that text becomes the whole bucket.
The discriminating probe (safe, zero-leak, and the next concrete step): open a compose via draft_email, then for each element matching that selector list print the tag, the aria-label, the valueText LENGTH, and a boolean for whether the text contains @. Mechanism 1 shows a non-empty bucket with no @; mechanism 2 shows an unexpected aria-label winning. Neither requires printing an address.
Likely blast radius beyond W4.1. The same warning appeared for W4.4 in the run logs (recipient assertion mismatch), and the assertion is shared with the confirmed-dispatch path — hasExplicitRecipientAssertions / validateConfirmedSendArgs read the same helpers. If the read-back cannot see a chip, the two-gate dispatch guard may be refusing on the same false negative. Do not loosen the assertion to make the row pass. It is a safety gate on the highest-harm action in the product; the fix belongs in the read-back so it can recognise a resolved chip, with the strictness preserved. Any change here needs a row proving the assertion still REFUSES a genuine mismatch.
Evidence. Four run logs and per-run artifacts from 2026-09-07 (/tmp/tick17-evalD.log and /tmp/v2-eval-results-*.json; not promoted to docs/evidence/ because row notes can carry message subjects and a redaction pass is owed first). The row now surfaces the driver's message instead of dropping it, which is how this was diagnosed at all — scripts/v2-eval.ts W4.1, commit 2005b13c.
Filed by the GA sprint driver at the agent ceiling: Backlog, not Ready. No fix attempted this tick — the tick's bounded action was CLWX-119.

**Comments (2):**

- GA blocker record - 2026-09-08. Outlook compose-boundary blocker status recorded in docs/evidence/GA_BLOCKER_RESOLUTION_2026-09-08.md. - Original blocker: draft_email falsely rejected a draft its own fill path created because readback did not recognize rendered Outlook compose content. - Source/local repair: rendered text readback plus exact active-compose To/Cc/Bcc bucket signals; 99 focused tests and one local signed-in synthetic draft check passed without clicking Send. - Current proof gap: installed Windows signed-in saved-reopen/read/draft acceptance remains pending and cannot run on the VM until Microsoft sign-in and current candidate installation are available. Keep the strict dispatch assertion; acceptance needs proof that true recipient mismatches still refuse.
- CLWX-123 source repair is independently reviewed CLEAR. Live DOM shape showed that Outlook rendered the requested newline correctly while textContent concatenated adjacent HTML blocks. Shared verification/readback now prefers rendered text; original native body typing is unchanged. Recipient proof now uses exact active-compose To/Cc/Bcc bucket signals, excludes suggestion/chrome decoys, accepts repeated same-bucket labels and rejects conflicting labels. Focused Outlook safety suite: 99 tests passed; typecheck and targeted lint passed. Communication replay/comparison passed. Signed-in local product-path diagnostic created one owned synthetic multiline draft: recipient, subject and normalized body matched; Send was not clicked. Saved-draft reopen activation remains unproven. This is source/local evidence, not installed Windows acceptance. Card remains In Progress; the next native moe.21 build must be installed and tested before closure.

Original defect and acceptance retained below

CLWX-FIRST-RESPONSE-RCA-20260908. Release blocker; RCA and repair active. Related: CLWX-22 release acceptance, CLWX-95 runtime recovery, CLWX-117 terminal turn behavior.

Reproduction: Install moe.24 source b814f804036fc2f9f32d3326c8694f4ae2ef7805, run 34189597051, into the standard-user Server 2022 profile; apply supported Online provisioning; launch the installed shortcut; wait for the enabled Online composer; create a new chat; submit the account-free acceptance token prompt. Installer and EXE/ASAR identity pass. First turn fails NO_RESPONSE after 180 seconds, shows an empty conversation and a generic busy banner.

Why prior success is not equivalent: The established VM profile on moe.22 a4efc7e4 answered in 85.090 seconds at 03:38:15Z with 30 seconds of terminal stability. The fresh profile on the same moe.22 already failed: its Online answer was persisted 170.680 seconds after send, while the UI falsely degraded. Profiles and configured broker endpoints differ. These are not controlled version-only comparisons. moe.24 removes the false silent fallback/replay; it does not remove cold initialization.

Observed failure boundary: Send click 06:11:28.506Z; Gateway session initialization 06:11:30.601Z; MoE tools registered 06:11:59Z. Runtime trajectory reaches session.started 06:13:20.665Z and prompt.submitted 06:13:20.789Z, then model.completed 06:13:20.807Z with externalAbort=true, aborted=true, timedOut=true, idleTimedOut=false. The session index exists but its transcript file does not. The model idle timeout is configured to 600 seconds. In this runtime, a normal abort can be classified as timeout; timedOut does not prove the producer of cancellation.

Cloud evidence: The bounded broker access window contains GET models HTTP200 in 0.956 seconds and POST chat/completions HTTP200 beginning 06:13:21.080Z with 37.222 seconds total request duration. This is time/route correlation, not a propagated turn ID, and HTTP200 alone does not prove an answer reached the app. No 401/403/429 is observed in these access records.

Source-supported mechanism under investigation: The renderer aborts accepted runs after 90 seconds without owned progress. Cold preparation can exceed that budget. History replacement preserves the optimistic prompt only during active sending; timeout clears those ownership fields. The no-response string mentions quota, which maps to misleading busy copy. The current driver checks run errors and error chips but missed this generic error banner. The separate startup history/catalog wait completed before this prompt and is not established as the sole cause.

Required diagnosis and repair: Bind cancellation producer and preparation cost to source; compare relevant upstream and last-working revisions; retain no-duplicate/no-false-fallback behavior. Add a flow map with observed versus unverified boundaries, controlled experiments, provenance and environment/setup instructions.
